# Open questions & assumptions

Every ambiguity found while writing the docs, with the assumption made so work never blocked on it. Assumptions are also marked inline (`**Assumption:**`) where they occur. Owner is Stefan unless noted.

**Current pricing values (action classes, packs, signup grant) live in [09-pricing.md](09-pricing.md) — #3/#4/#58 below keep the decision history, not the reference numbers.**

> **Closed rows live in [open-questions-archive.md](open-questions-archive.md).** When a numbered row is missing from the table below, it was
> moved there because it is terminally closed (done/built/shipped/rejected/decided with nothing left open). **Numbering is global and never**
> **reused** — a `[#NN](open-questions.md)` link anywhere may refer to an archived row; check the archive when the number isn't here.
>
> **Historical PR-number citations (`PR #NN`) throughout this doc and the archive may 404** (found session 87, 2026-09-07, during a full
> re-triage): `Stefan7168/checkdecijfers` on GitHub was created 2026-09-05 (`gh repo view`; `gh pr list --state all` shows only PRs #1–#6) —
> older PR-number citations point at a prior GitHub repo instance that no longer exists (consistent with the documented account/repo-recreation
> history). The underlying **commit SHAs remain independently verifiable** (`git log`) and every SHA spot-checked during the re-triage was
> confirmed present on `main` — a dead PR-number link is a citation-format issue, not a reason to doubt the row's build/merge claim.

| 14 | GDPR | Retention period for question logs / audit records once accounts exist; anonymization approach. | **Decided (Stefan, 2026-07-04, session 18): 2-year retention, and a user can delete their own question history.** **Built (WP14, 2026-07-05, code-only/hermetic session):** `npm run gdpr:purge` (`scripts/gdpr-purge.ts`, dry-run by default, `--apply` to run for real) redacts `source_tag='user'` `audit_answers` rows older than 2 years; a "Verwijder mijn vraaggeschiedenis" button (account panel) calls a `getClaims()`-verified server action (`deleteMyQuestionHistory`, `web/app/actions.ts`) that redacts the CALLING user's own `source_tag='user'` rows, any age — one click + an inline confirmation step (owner decision, session 23; not a typed-word confirmation). **Load-bearing implementation choice, discovered mid-build:** both paths **redact** (`UPDATE` — overwrite `question`/`final_text`/`response`/`reply_text`/`pending_clarification` with a fixed sentinel) rather than physically `DELETE`, because `credit_transactions.audit_answer_id`'s FK (migration 005, deliberately no `ON DELETE` clause) throws on a hard delete of any clarification/refusal row a compensation entry references — confirmed empirically in `tests/audit/retention.test.ts`. This is also a strictly better match for the owner's UX decision (a placeholder row that keeps its id and ledger join): the dashboard now renders a "verwijderde vraag" placeholder (`web/components/question-history.tsx`) with the credit amount intact, question/answer text gone, row never hidden. No schema change. Hermetic pins (14 tests, `tests/audit/retention.test.ts`) cover: deletion scoped to the calling user only (another user's rows byte-identical survive — the critical security pin), the ledger never written by either path, the purge never touching benchmark/validation-tagged rows, purge idempotency, and the FK-violation proof. Full gate green (714 backend + 135 web tests, benchmark 14/14+6/6+0 fabricated, clean typecheck both sides, real `next build`). **Still open:** (d) a privacy policy, which doesn't exist yet and is a separate, larger piece of Phase 1's GDPR checklist. **Stale-note fix (session 87, 2026-09-07, found during a full re-triage): the "purge never run against production" clause above is outdated** — a monthly Vercel cron has run `gdpr:purge` since 2026-07-26, and since 2026-09-05 (`GDPR_PURGE_APPLY=1`, session 78) it actually enforces retention (verified: 0 rows, matching the dry-run) rather than only reporting — see [#189](open-questions.md). |
| 17 | Pricing | Scoop alerts: subscription (~€29/mo per the notes) vs. credit-funded — an individual subscription sits in tension with the "no subscription, no fine print" promise. | Slotted Phase 3 with the pricing model explicitly undecided; re-decide at Phase 3 design |
| 21 | Product | Demand-driven table onboarding (Phase 2–3): may a freshly fetched table answer immediately using auto-generated "provisional" mappings, or only after mapping review? | Review-first assumed (ingest now, answer after mapping review, notify the user) — instant-provisional rejected for now because unreviewed mappings risk confident wrong answers, the failure principle (a) exists to prevent. **Revised (Stefan, 2026-07-04):** the original framing implied a *person* reviews each table — challenged as unnecessary, correctly: what actually catches mapping errors is *independent verification*, not human judgment, and the project's own strongest example (the werkloosheid seasonally-adjusted/yearly mismatch, ADR [019](decisions/019-open-ended-period-ranges.md) §4) was in fact caught by a **structural consistency check** (does this coordinate combination exist with real published data), not by anyone eyeballing StatLine. **New design — fully automated, no manual click:** an onboarding pipeline runs (a) a structural check — does the candidate coordinate combination have real, non-null data across a representative period range; (b) a label-consistency check — does CBS's own published dimension/measure label text match the concept being registered; (c) an independent cross-check against a second, differently-sourced reference figure where one is available. **High confidence on all three → the table auto-graduates from `needs_review`, zero manual click** (satisfies the existing rule in [05-data-rules.md](05-data-rules.md): "mapping review means passing those tasks; nothing else counts" — the tasks' frozen-key values now come from this automated verification, not a person). **Low confidence, or genuinely multiple valid CBS definitions with no clear default → never silently pick one and never refuse: show each candidate's real, correctly-attributed value with its own definition label**, adopting Stefan's proposal directly ("hier zijn de gerelateerde cijfers, zo kun je het van beide kanten bekijken") — related to [#39](open-questions.md) (deferred for *existing* tables; worth revisiting there too). **Residual risk, named not hidden:** an automated check can still miss an error that exists identically in our ingested copy *and* whatever independent source it's checked against (a true shared blind spot) — narrower than "no check at all," and further caught by the disclosure fallback in most remaining cases; accepted as a monitored trade-off, revisit if it ever produces a measured wrong high-confidence answer. **Placeholder queued in [08-build-plan.md](08-build-plan.md) as WP16 (working number)** so this decision surfaces to a session reading only the build plan, not just here |

Slice CPI (like `03759ned`) or budget the paid tier before Phase 1 adds tables? | **Decided** (Stefan, 2026-07-04): slice the CPI table down now rather than pay for a bigger tier. **Not yet buildable as a same-session flip** — [#34](open-questions.md) already flags that the tooling to safely *change* a registered slice doesn't exist yet (needs a reviewed update + rebaseline flow); queued as a small scoped engineering task, not done in this session |
| 34 | Engineering | Ingestion follow-ups observed at first live ingest, deferred to the registry work package: (a) no path exists yet to *change* a registered slice (needs a reviewed update + rebaseline flow); (b) syncs are slow for their size (4–6 min for small tables) because dimension-label writes go row-by-row over the wire — batch them; (c) concurrent syncs of one table aren't locked — add an advisory lock before Phase 1 scheduled syncs. | None of these affect correctness today (single operator, manual CLI); tracked so they're built deliberately, not rediscovered. **⚠ RE-VERIFIED against current code (session 63, 2026-08-26/27) — the "single operator, manual CLI" premise no longer held.** (b) confirmed STILL OPEN: `src/ingestion/pipeline.ts` writes `dimension_labels` via a sequential per-row `INSERT` loop at all three write sites (`registerTables` lines 104-115, the rebaseline path, `acceptNewCodes`) — the SAME function already knows how to batch (`observations` writes chunk 5000-at-a-time via `jsonb_to_recordset`), just never applied that pattern here. **(c) ✅ THE CONFIRMED CRASH/CORRUPTION RISK IS FIXED** (`syncTable`'s rebaseline branch: `DELETE` + un-guarded per-row `INSERT` into `dimension_labels`, the one write in the transaction with no unique-constraint/ON CONFLICT protection, unlike every other write there): a `pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtext(tableId))` now serializes concurrent rebaselines of the same table, scoped to just the rebaseline branch (not the whole transaction, so ordinary same-table syncs — already upsert-safe — never pay the wait) and bounded by a `lock_timeout` (this pool sets none anywhere else, and an unbounded wait could tie up a pooled connection past the onboarding cron's 300s Vercel ceiling, turning a clean failure into a killed function). Pinned by `tests/ingestion/ingestion.test.ts`'s `#34(c)` test (with the same PGlite-single-connection-mutex caveat `tests/billing/ledger.test.ts`'s own concurrent-debit tests already carry — hermetic tests can't exercise real multi-connection concurrency, so this rests on `pg_advisory_xact_lock` being a standard, well-understood Postgres primitive, reasoned about at the code level). **Two DEEPER findings from the same review, confirmed real, deliberately NOT fixed here — need their own scoped session, not a rushed addition to this fix:** (i) **TOCTOU on pre-lock validation reads** — the five validators (schema fingerprint, dimension-mapping's `storedLabels` read, etc.) all run via plain `db.query` *before* `db.withTransaction` even opens, so a concurrent rebaseline can change what they validated against between the read and the eventual write; this window is pre-existing (not newly introduced by the lock) and the lock alone doesn't close it — closing it needs either moving validation inside the lock or a post-lock re-validation step. (ii) **Silent clobber via the rebaseline's unguarded version bump** — `update cbs_tables set ... version = version + 1 ... where id = $1` has no `WHERE version = $expected` optimistic-concurrency guard, so two concurrent rebaselines now serialize cleanly (thanks to the lock) but the SECOND one to acquire the lock can silently commit its own (possibly stale-validated, per (i)) baseline over the first's fresher one — both report `outcome: 'succeeded'`, no error, no indication anything was overwritten. Recommended fix shape for a future session: capture the registry `version` read at validation time, re-read it immediately after acquiring the lock, and throw a loud "modified concurrently, retry" error (via the existing `failBatch` convention, not a bare throw, so the `ingestion_batches` row doesn't get left stuck at `'running'`) if it moved. **✅ (2026-08-27, session 66, autonomous): (b) + (c)(i) + (c)(ii) ALL BUILT** — branch `fix/34-ingestion-pipeline-hardening`, PR #100 awaiting owner review per #118(b); (a) remains open. **(b):** all three `dimension_labels` write sites (registration, rebaseline rewrite, acceptNewCodes) now insert in `CHUNK_SIZE` batches via `jsonb_to_recordset` — the same pattern the observations staging write already used; new tests pin the written rows content-identical to the fetched code lists (incl. a >5000-code multi-chunk case), and each site's conflict behavior is preserved exactly (the two `on conflict do nothing` sites keep it; the delete-then-rewrite deliberately keeps NO on-conflict, so a duplicate code inside one CBS code list stays a loud unique-violation instead of a silent drop). **(c)(ii):** after `pg_advisory_xact_lock`, the rebaseline re-reads `cbs_tables.version` (`for update`) and compares against the pre-lock read; moved ⇒ abort via a purpose-built `RebaselineAbortError` thrown inside the transaction (rolled back by `withTransaction`'s contract), caught in `syncTable`'s outer scope, and recorded through `failBatch` as the new failure stage `rebaseline_conflict` — no quarantine, since the concurrent winner's baseline is freshly validated, not suspect. Requires **migration 022** (extends the `failure_stage` CHECK constraint) — hermetic tests apply it automatically; **the live apply is one supervised `src/db/migrate.ts` run at merge/deploy**; until it runs, the race stays data-safe (transaction still rolls back) but the batch record degrades to the pre-existing stuck-`'running'` shape instead of a clean `'failed'`. **(c)(i):** scoped to the rebaseline path per this row's own analysis — under rebaseline, stages 1/3/4/5 validate the freshly fetched schema/codes/units against themselves, so only stage 2's inputs (`last_row_count`, `row_count_tolerance`) come from the pre-lock registry read; the same post-lock re-read re-runs `checkRowPlausibility` against the fresh values and aborts with a quarantine (identical semantics to any stage-2 failure) when a concurrent ordinary sync's committed row count invalidates the stale read. Ordinary (non-rebaseline) syncs deliberately untouched (upsert-safe, per this row). 5 new hermetic tests: content-equivalence ×2, a deterministic pre-transaction-interference harness for (c)(i) and (c)(ii), and the two-concurrent-rebaselines race (exactly one wins; the loser fails `rebaseline_conflict`; version bumps once; both batch rows terminal). Known residue, deliberately unchanged: a mid-write infrastructure error (e.g. a genuinely duplicate CBS code) still propagates and leaves the batch `'running'` — pre-existing `syncTable` behavior for infrastructure failures, out of this fix's scope. **✅ PR #100 merged into `main`, session 67 (2026-08-28, `c02f126`).** **Migration 022 APPLIED to production 2026-09-02 (session 69, owner present, together with 023/024/025 in one `npm run db:migrate`; `schema_migrations` re-checked 2026-09-03) — the "supervised apply at merge/deploy" sentence above is history now. Noted 2026-09-03 (session 72).** |
| 38 | Engineering | The WP5 intent contract supports one varying axis per question: several periods at one place, or several regions at one period — never both (no "compare the G4's growth 2019–2024" in one intent). | **Assumption** (2026-07-03, query WP5; ADR [011](decisions/011-query-contract.md)): B1–B14 need at most one varying axis, so the cross-product shape is out of contract (`invalid_intent`) rather than half-supported. Revisit trigger: a real question shape that needs both, at WP6 calibration or benchmark revision — extend the contract + `INTENT_SCHEMA_VERSION` then. **Superseded for this row's own example (session 110, 2026-09-17, ADR [055](decisions/055-multi-region-series.md)): "compare the G4's growth 2019–2024" is now ANSWERED** — a handful of explicitly named regions (2–6, `REGION_SERIES_MAX_REGIONS`) crossed with a period range is a new `'region_series'` result shape, derived from the existing `regions`/`period` fields with no contract change and no `INTENT_SCHEMA_VERSION` bump. The one-varying-axis rule still holds for everything else: a region CLASS crossed with a range (ADR [054](decisions/054-region-set-query.md)'s `regionSet`) still refuses `multi_region_multi_period`, as does more than 6 named regions or a cross-product over 500 cells. This row's revisit trigger is therefore partially fired, not fully: the general cross-product-with-a-region-CLASS case (this trigger's own remaining scope) still needs a contract/version change if it's ever wanted. |
| 43 | Engineering | Staleness thresholds (docs/05 failure-table row, implemented WP9): when does "table past its expected update cadence" trip? | **Assumption, now live** (2026-07-03, WP9; ADR [015](decisions/015-refusal-clarification-composition.md)): cadence-string prefix → max age = expected cadence × 1.5 (monthly → 47 days, quarterly → 138, yearly → 549), measured against our `last_sync_at` with the pipeline's injected reference date; unknown cadence strings make no staleness claim. Constants in [src/answer/respond/staleness.ts](../src/answer/respond/staleness.ts), owner-tunable. The margin absorbs CBS's "no fixed publication times"; basis is *our sync age*, not CBS's own publication lag — revisit if a missed-CBS-publication signal is ever ingested |
| 45 | Product | Chart embedding in third-party websites (Phase 2+). Should users be able to take a chart beyond our own pages — and if so, how? | Owner's initial position (2026-07-04): embedding is a paid feature (costs extra credits), with two delivery shapes to evaluate: (a) download as a standalone file (SVG for print/vector quality, high-res PNG for platforms that need raster) and (b) a live embed (iframe or whatever mechanism proves best at design time). Trade-off to weigh deliberately: embeds keep visitors on the third party's site, while the already-planned landing-page share links (#9) bring visitors to ours — pricing embeds higher partly compensates for that lost traffic. Technically cheap either way: every renderer draws from the same chart spec (ADR 007), the WP8 SVG renderer is already reserved as the static-export path (ADR 014), and credit pricing plugs into the pricing-config seam (ADR 006). Decide at the Phase 2 sharing/design session, together with #9 and the social-export template (#29). **Cross-reference (owner, 2026-07-04):** if chart toggles (#46) ship before this does, exported/embedded artifacts must not silently carry a visitor's toggled/customized view — see #46(c) for the reasoning and the reputational-risk concern; design both together if their timing overlaps. **Built, session 93 (2026-09-10), branch `embed-charts`, not yet merged — ADR [041](decisions/041-public-embed-pages.md):** the session-92 owner decisions superseded shape (b) above — the live tier is not a credit-priced "live embed," it is **Live embed = Pro** (a plain email allowlist today, `PRO_ACCOUNT_EMAILS`, pending the real #205 plan); shape (a) (download as a standalone file) already existed via the chart download menu. The new ladder: free = a frozen embed (signed, stateless `/embed/[token]` route) with attribution + backlink; paid = Live embed, Pro-gated. **The live tier cannot actually turn on for anyone yet** — see [#224](open-questions.md) and ADR 041's Consequences/Revisit triggers for why (a missing user-id-to-email lookup, not a billing decision). **As-built, 2026-09-12:** the lookup is now built; see #224's resolution — Live still needs one owner-run verification query against the live project before it is observable. |
| 49 | Product | Corrections vs. exported/embedded charts: #22 already covers our own pages (freeze as an as-of snapshot + correction banner). An iframe embed on someone else's site is live and would silently change under their article if CBS later corrects the data; a downloaded SVG is frozen and would go quietly stale instead. | **Resolved** (Stefan, 2026-07-04): live embeds (iframes) auto-update when CBS corrects the underlying data; downloaded files freeze at export time. Matches how each format naturally behaves. Both must visibly carry their own date/status so neither promise is silent — implement when the embedding feature itself is built ([#45](open-questions.md)). **Confirmed as built, session 93 (2026-09-10), ADR [041](decisions/041-public-embed-pages.md):** a Live embed re-runs the row's stored query and shows a footer reading "Live · data as of {date}"; a frozen embed's footer reads "Frozen on {date} · checkdecijfers.nl" and never changes after minting — neither promise is silent. **Note:** today every embed is effectively frozen regardless of the Live toggle's setting — the Pro check that would turn Live on has no way yet to identify an embed's owner ([#224](open-questions.md)) — so this row's "live embeds auto-update" half is built and tested but not yet observable in practice. **As-built, 2026-09-12:** the owner-email lookup is now built (#224) — this half becomes observable once the owner runs the RUNBOOK's one verification query against the live project. |

| 59 | Legal/Engineering | WP13's `credit_transactions` FK to `auth.users(id)` deliberately has **no `on delete cascade`** (ADR [020](decisions/020-credit-ledger-and-billing-gate.md) decision 5) — cascading a user deletion would silently contradict the append-only, lives-forever design of a financial ledger. But no account-deletion feature exists yet, so the tension is real but theoretical today: the day one is built, a deleted user's `auth.users` row cannot actually be removed while ledger rows reference it (the FK blocks it by default), and simply detaching the ledger from the user (nulling `user_id`) isn't legitimate either — the ledger's whole point is knowing whose balance is whose. | **Not decided — flagged, not resolved.** Candidate approaches for whenever account deletion is designed: (a) anonymize the ledger's link (keep the rows, replace `user_id` with a tombstone value — breaks append-only's "never edit" rule for exactly one column, a real cost); (b) never truly delete the `auth.users` row, only deactivate it (a common pattern that sidesteps the FK entirely); (c) export-then-delete (give the user their ledger history, then genuinely erase, accepting the ledger's completeness invariant is scoped to "while the account exists"). Needs a GDPR-erasure-vs-financial-record-retention legal read, not just an engineering call — same flavor as [#5](open-questions.md)'s "credits never expire" wording check |

a B-defaulted answer's question chips now name the country (the take is an explicit national intent that discloses no default); a stripped carrier's fresh parse records role `intent` (#177); the "still merges" claim scoped to direct calls with the boundary pinned as the deployed gate; a send whose captured thread is null (its turn's attach had failed) falls back to the live thread — a chip take or a typed clarification reply alike (no forked thread; ADR 033 ⟨A6⟩ addendum). **Follow-ups:** the carriers are keyed by message index in a ref (correct today, an implicit coupling) — move the carrier onto `ChatMessage` once `web/lib/chat-message.ts` is free (after PR #123); and with the live-thread fallback the ⟨A6⟩ `capturedThreadId` (+ the carrier's `threadId`) is provably the live thread or null at send time — a later cleanup can pass the live thread on both paths and drop both fields (ADR 033 ⟨A6⟩ addendum). **✅ BOTH BUILT (session 76, 2026-09-04, branch `fix/73-carrier-on-chatmessage`):** `ChatMessage` (`web/lib/chat-message.ts`) now carries `carrier: { pending: PendingClarification } | null` directly, set in the SAME `setMessages` call that appends the message (never a render behind); `chat.tsx`'s index-keyed `carriersRef` Map is gone, and the chip click handler binds to `message.carrier` off the exact message rendered, preserving byte-for-byte the "own message's carrier, never the newest" behavior for two answers sharing a label (already pinned in `chat.test.tsx`'s "chip on an OLDER answer" case, unchanged and still green). `web/lib/replay-assemble.ts`'s three message builders all set `carrier: null` — the type system now forces every replay site to do this, rather than relying on a convention (ADR 033 ⟨A6⟩: carriers are not restored on resume). Confirmed the `capturedThreadId`/carrier-`threadId` equivalence (traced every place `threadId` state and a captured/carrier value are set — both are always written from the SAME `outcome.threadId` in the turn that produced them, and a thread switch clears `pending`/every carrier together with resetting `threadId` itself, so a surviving captured value can never diverge from the live one) and dropped both fields; `chat.tsx` now sends the live `threadId` state directly on the typed-reply AND the chip-take path. Measure first: how often real onboarding requests land on such tables (the pending rows + `failure_summary` record it) — widen on evidence, per the ADR 025 pattern. **⬆ ELEVATED to a real priority — owner-flagged at the go-live (session 28, 2026-07-06):** the very FIRST live onboarding — *"Hoeveel mensen zaten er in 2023 in de bijstand?"* — hit this: finder confidently picked `85615NED`, delivery returned `unanswerable` ("geen maat die we onder deze vraag konden aanbieden"), 100 credits refunded. The money-safety worked perfectly, BUT the owner's verdict (verbatim, binding): *"dat is er eentje die echt wel beantwoord moet kunnen worden … we kunnen niet, als iemand zo'n simpele vraag stelt, zeggen dat het ons niet lukt … dit kan echt niet."* Bijstand is a common, simple, national question and MUST answer. **Diagnosis (CBS metadata checked, session 28): `85615NED` is a FINDER MIS-PICK, not primarily a delivery gap.** `85615NED` = *"Personen met bijstand; (Her)instromers en uitstromers"* — it measures INFLOWS/OUTFLOWS (entry/exit moments), broken down by gender/age/origin, quarterly. It has no stock "how many people ARE in bijstand" total — so for *"hoeveel mensen zaten er in de bijstand"* (a stock/count question) delivery correctly found no fitting measure and refused. **The real fix lever is (a) FINDER PRECISION:** "hoeveel mensen in de bijstand" must map to a STOCK/count table (e.g. a *"Bijstandsuitkeringen; kerncijfers"* / *"Personen met (algemene) bijstand"* national-total table), NOT the flows table. The rerank matched the topic word "bijstand" but ignored stock-vs-flow intent. **⚠ OWNER STEER (session 28, binding): do NOT solve this statically per-topic — "we kunnen niet voor elke vraag ditzelfde probleem statisch gaan oplossen; het moet gewoon slimmer worden aangepakt."** So the fix is NOT per-topic aliases (bijstand→stock table, then repeat for every subject — that doesn't scale to the ~4,858-table catalog). It is a GENERAL capability: **the rerank must reason about each candidate table's actual SHAPE — its measure names + dimensions (stock vs flow, total vs breakdown, level vs change) — against the question's intent, not just topic-word overlap.** Today the rerank sees mostly title/Description; it should also see the table's measures/dimensions so "meet in-/uitstroom" vs "meet aantal personen" is visible and scorable. **Plus a fit-check BEFORE spending 100 credits:** the stock-vs-flow mismatch is currently only discovered at DELIVERY (after fetch + debit → refund); a pre-fetch "does this table actually contain a measure that answers this question?" gate could reject/try-next-candidate before charging. This is a design-heavy core-product question (intent↔table-shape matching + a pre-fetch fit gate) — a legitimate **Fable** candidate (owner authorized Fable on WP16's hard sub-parts). Secondary (b): the general #111 delivery-coverage widening still applies for genuinely region/characteristic-split tables. **Session 30 (2026-07-07): the pre-design PROBLEM DOSSIER is written ([session-briefs/2026-07-07-111-problem-dossier.md](session-briefs/2026-07-07-111-problem-dossier.md)) — key verified fact: `findTable` receives only the parser's `unmatchedMeasureTerm` ("bijstand"), so the stock-vs-flow intent is discarded BEFORE the finder runs; a rerank-prompt-only fix cannot work. | 127 | Ops / cost | **Should we enable Anthropic prompt caching (`cache_control`) on our LLM calls?** Owner spotted the console's "Prompt caching — Not enabled" nudge (2026-07-11, session 36). | **Answered: technically possible, DEFERRED — not useful at today's scale.** Four measured reasons: (1) our static prompt prefixes (few hundred tokens) sit BELOW the minimum cacheable prefix (~1,024 tokens), so most calls cannot cache at all; (2) the cache TTL is ~5 min and the owner is the only user — mostly-miss traffic pays the +25% cache-WRITE premium and saves nothing; (3) adding `cache_control` changes request bytes → every recorded LLM fixture re-keys → full re-record with live spend (against the zero-prompt-byte/hermetic discipline) unless the fixture hasher is first taught to ignore it; (4) current live LLM spend is cents — 50–90% of ~nothing is ~nothing. **Revisit trigger (matches the existing [04-architecture](04-architecture.md) scale table, which already names prompt caching at ~1,000 active users):** real question volume or a much larger static prefix; then FIRST make the fixture hasher ignore `cache_control`, then enable. |

| 140 | Data integrity / core promise | **CRITICAL — metadata-number whitelist bypass in the anti-fabrication validator (`src/answer/compose/validate.ts`), found by the session-44 data-integrity hunt (two independent lenses) and OWNER-flagged core-risk.** `buildAllowedNumbers` pooled every DIGIT found anywhere in metadata prose (definitionLabel, periodSemantics, measureTitle, regionLabel, dimLabels) and `scanBody` exempted ANY body number equal to one from the R9/R10 binding + unit checks — with NO context. So a fabricated value that merely COINCIDED with a digit buried in metadata (the "2024" inside a "2024JJ00" period code, the "100" inside an index base "2015=100") passed validation as "backed". Reproduced end-to-end (a fabricated "2024 euro" per-person figure returned `ok:true, problems:[]`); reconstruct.ts R8 re-runs the same validator so it couldn't catch it either. | **✅ LARGELY FIXED (v3) — session 44, branch `fix/validator-metadata-fabrication-hole`; owner chose (2026-07-16) to ship the big narrowing now + track the residual.** New `metadataNumberAnchors`/`tokenContext` (format.ts) pair each metadata number with the word beside it in the SOURCE; `metadataEcho` (validate.ts) exempts a body number only when it matches AND reappears next to that same word through a DISTINCTIVE anchor (letter-bearing, non-stopword — a bare numeral like "2015"/"000" is never an anchor) on one side, OR the same word on both sides; `periodSemantics` guidance prose is STRICT (both sides must match). **Three adversarial-review rounds hardened it:** a single shared word was too weak ("in 2024" via the stopword "in"); bare-numeral anchors laundered CBS space-grouped labels ("20 000 tot 30 000 euro") + index bases ("(2015=100)"); a single generic domain noun ("45 jaar", "inkomen 20") still bound. **KNOWN BOUNDED RESIDUAL (deterministic ceiling):** a fabricated number that EQUALS one of the result's OWN descriptor-coordinate numbers (an age/income-bracket value, "1 januari") AND sits next to that descriptor's own word can still pass — the legit coordinate echo and such a fabrication are textually IDENTICAL, so no deterministic text rule separates them. The strict "both-sides for ALL sources" version that would catch it BROKE 4 legit stored answers (measured R8-reconstruct regressions: B6 factor-unit + web-augmentation), so it is not viable. This is a large narrowing of the original "any metadata digit exempts, no context" hole (which is live in `main` today), NOT a full close. **Residual → needs a SEMANTIC-level check (a follow-up, not a deterministic tweak); see [#144](open-questions.md).** Closes end-to-end: period codes, index bases, bracket numerals, connector words. Full validator + benchmark suite green (1289 backend); repro'd every exploit + the fix. |

| 173 | Ops / infra | **Supabase free tier caps SESSION-mode pooling at 15 connections, and a burst of deploys exhausts it — MEASURED live 2026-07-25 (session 56).** After five production deploys within ~an hour, `(EMAXCONNSESSION) max clients reached in session mode - max clients are limited to pool_size: 15` was returned to both a local script and production functions. Observed for ~6 minutes: `/llms.txt` served its honest 503 fail-safe, and the homepage Ontdek charts were omitted (`/` still 200). It SELF-HEALED as the extra function instances went away — ⚠ **"went idle" was the original wording and it is MEASURED WRONG (2026-07-26, [#186](open-questions.md)): a pooler session survived 174 s of idleness, because node-pg's 10 s `idleTimeoutMillis` does not fire while a Fluid Compute instance is frozen. The slot returns on instance TEARDOWN, not on the idle timer** — which is why recovery takes minutes rather than seconds. Both surfaces degraded exactly as designed — no stale or invented data — so this is a CAPACITY issue, not a correctness one. Each function instance opens its own pg pool; laptop scripts (`audit:verify`, `catalog:refresh`) draw from the same 15. ⚠ Bearing on WP26: both answer-first mechanisms add DB work per request (B-region an existence probe, B-period a window query), so the go-live should not happen during a deploy burst. | **Options:** (a) accept + operate around it (documented in the RUNBOOK now: don't stack deploys, don't run laptop scripts in a deploy window, `/llms.txt` is the cheapest canary); (b) switch the app to TRANSACTION-mode pooling, which multiplexes far more clients per connection — needs a check that nothing relies on session state (prepared statements, advisory locks: the billing gate DOES use a per-user advisory lock, so this needs care); (c) reduce the per-instance pool size (`max`) in the pg client so each function holds fewer; (d) paid tier (raises, does not remove, the ceiling). **Recommendation: (c) then (b)** — (c) is a one-line, low-risk change that multiplies headroom; (b) is the real fix but must not break the advisory-lock path. Owner call, not urgent while traffic is the owner alone. **▶ (c) DONE + MERGED + LIVE (session 57, 2026-07-25, PR #60 squash `e334590`, CI + deploy green, production verified 200 after the deploy): `src/db/client.ts` caps each process at 2 pooler sessions instead of 4 — four busy processes could exhaust the project alone, now it takes eight. Pinned by `tests/db/pool-config.test.ts`. Verified safe for the advisory-lock path first: all 14 `withTransaction` call sites use only the `tx` they are given, none nests, and nothing needs two clients at once, so a small pool can only queue work, never deadlock it. `connectionTimeoutMillis` deliberately left at the node-pg default — a bounded wait could throw between a committed debit and its compensating refund, which is a supervised money-path change, not a capacity tweak. ▶ (b) transaction-mode pooling REMAINS OPEN and is still the real fix: it must first be checked against `pg_advisory_xact_lock` (billing gate + onboarding trigger) and the session-scoped temp tables the ingestion staging step uses.** |
| 174 | WP26 / trust boundary | **`ClickOption.impliedRecency` is a CLIENT-held policy bit that decides whether a stale table refuses or serves — found by the adversarial review, 2026-07-25 (session 57), and deliberately NOT fixed there.** `validate-pending.ts` type-checks it (`z.boolean()`) and `clickTakeOutcome` copies it straight into the parse outcome, where it is the sole input to the docs/05 staleness *refusal* branch (`respond.ts`: `if (staleness.stale && parse.impliedRecency)`). A forged `false` on a stale table turns a refusal into a served figure. Latent — the take path only runs with `CLARIFY_CLICK_ENABLED` on. | **Assessed and left alone on purpose; the obvious fix is worse than the bug.** Re-deriving it as "the option's period reaches the freshest published period" is a DIFFERENT rule from what the offer side means (the user asked for "now"), and would make a legitimate chip for an explicitly-named historical period start refusing where it correctly warns-and-serves today — the rescue chip deliberately sets `impliedRecency: false` for exactly that reason. Forcing `true` has the same over-refusal problem. **Actual harm is small and self-inflicted:** the attacker is the user, the staleness warning still ships in the answer text, and the figure is a real traceable cell — nobody is misled, they just see a warned figure instead of a refusal. Wants a real decision about what the bit MEANS before code moves. Owner's call; not a go-live blocker. |

The kickoff brief's "`parse.ts` byte-identical" is a conservative shorthand for that. So threading `clickOptionsEnabled` into `resolveCandidate` (which runs AFTER the LLM call, on the parsed candidate) cannot invalidate a fixture as long as no prompt string moves — and the intent replay suite is the empirical check, since a changed request simply misses its fixture and throws. Shape confirmed by reading: `resolveCandidate` already takes an `options` bag (`{ answerFirstEnabled?: boolean }`, `resolve.ts:958`), the flag is already threaded to `policy.ts` from `parse.ts` and `followup.ts`, and the wasted work is the `regionOptionIntents` → `resolvePeriod` call at `resolve.ts:994`. **This is the clearly-next capacity item.** **✅ BUILT 2026-07-26 (session 60), design A.** `resolveCandidate`'s existing options bag gained `clickOptionsEnabled`; the `regionOptionIntents` call is gated on it; `parse.ts` and `followup.ts` pass the same flag they already pass to `decide()`, and `clarify.ts` deliberately passes nothing — the reply turn is the final round and never asks again, so per-option intents were dead work there in **every** flag state, which the change also removes. Design B (move the construction into `policy.ts`'s gated branch) was **rejected on review**: it would need `ResolutionFailure` to carry `optionCodes` + the period spec + the canonical row out of the resolver and a third injected callback through `decide()`'s signature, all to keep `policy.ts` DB-free — and it would have to re-create one subtlety exactly or silently change flag-ON behavior (`regionOptionIntents` calls `resolvePeriod` WITHOUT `answerFirstEnabled` and without region codes, `resolve.ts:933`, so a period-less ambiguous question builds no chips even fully flag-on; deliberate-looking but recorded nowhere until now). **⚠ THE FINDING THAT MATTERED MOST, and it was in the test file, not the source:** `tests/answer/query-count.test.ts` carried a case labelled *"the shape #176 was found on"* — and it was not. It passes `regions: null`, which returns ok early at `resolve.ts:166` and **never enters the failure branch #176 is about**; its `served:false` came from the query layer. So the fix moved **none** of the pre-existing pinned numbers, and a session that verified against that tripwire as it stood would have proved nothing — *a no-op gate would have stayed just as green*. Two new pins on the real shape (a NAMED ambiguous region) measure it: **flag-off 3 statements, flag-on 4** — the fix removes ~25% of the resolver's statements on that turn, and the label was corrected in the same change. **Mutation-verified, not assumed:** removing the gate fails the flag-off pin (3→4) and both absent-key tests; **inverting** it fails 10 tests across 3 files; dropping the `followup.ts` threading fails the new follow-up-chip test, which nothing covered before (the whole existing click suite went through `parse.ts` only — forgetting the second call site would have shipped green, and the failure mode is silent: the clarification renders perfectly, minus its chips). The `'max'`-derivation case is labelled a **guard, not a proof** — it passes with the gate removed too, since `regionOptionIntents` early-outs on `max` on its own. **Residual under flag-ON, deliberately NOT closed:** intents are still built for every region-ambiguous candidate among the up-to-3 resolved in parallel, while only the TOP one's are ever read — 1-2 statements each. ⚠ *A first draft of this row said that needs "several candidates simultaneously region-ambiguous"; the review corrected it, and the correction makes the residual more reachable, not less:* it happens whenever **any non-top** candidate is region-ambiguous, including a mixed parse whose top reading is a plain success (e.g. "bevolking Amsterdam 2024" at 0.95 alongside a bare "Utrecht" at 0.6 — the turn ANSWERS, and Utrecht's intents are built and never read, since `clarificationFromFailure` only ever sees a top-ranked failure and rule 4's chips come from the top/runner-up path at `policy.ts:495-524`). Still bounded and still not worth closing: 1-2 statements, dwarfed by the `echoServability` dry-run each offered chip already costs. Revisit on renewed #173 pooler pressure with the flag on, or a post-flip corpus showing multi-candidate parses at a non-trivial rate. |
| 178 | WP26 / state lifetime | **A client-held pending has no TTL, no user/thread binding and no enforced `version`, so a weeks-old one is still honoured** — three separate findings from the deploy-skew review, 2026-07-25 (session 57). Concrete harm at age: a chip minted for "wat is de werkloosheid **nu**?" stores concrete period codes; weeks later, after newer periods have synced, clicking it serves the old period with no staleness marker (`checkStaleness` measures table sync age, and the table is freshly synced). Separately, a stale tab can resurrect GDPR-erased question text into a NEW audit row after the user has run "Verwijder mijn vraaggeschiedenis". | **Accepted properties for now, recorded so they are not re-discovered.** The cross-user angle is a non-issue (an attacker must already hold the pending, it carries no values, and the audit row is written under the replaying user's own id). The two that deserve a decision are the silent "nu" staleness and the GDPR resurrection — both are user-initiated and self-healing, both are cheap to close (an age bound on the pending in `guardPending`, and re-comparing an `impliedRecency` chip's period against `freshestForCanonical` at take time). Owner's call on priority. **✅ The silent "nu"-staleness half is FIXED (session 63, autonomous; PR #95 merged 2026-08-27, `12e5799`): `clickOptionStillCurrent` (`src/answer/respond/respond.ts`) re-derives the current freshest period — at the option's own grain and region(s), not just the national default (a second bug the fix's own review caught) — before a click takes the zero-LLM-call fast path; a stale "nu" now falls through to the normal LLM merge instead of silently serving the old period. Known accepted gap, documented at the fix site: a `relative`-derived chip ("3 maanden geleden") is also `impliedRecency:true` but for a different reason (an unpublished-yet calendar point, not "wants latest"), so it will always miss the fast path too — safe (no wrong data, same fallback as any other reply) but a cost/latency regression for that one shape; `ClickOption` doesn't preserve which `PeriodSpec` produced a resolution, so this can't be fixed without a schema change. STILL OPEN, still owner's call on priority: the age-bound/TTL half (both the `guardPending` age bound and the GDPR-resurrection risk need one, and picking "how many days is too old" is the product judgment this row always reserved for the owner).** **Session 72 (2026-09-03, PR #122, merged): the chat client binds a chip click to its OWN message's carrier, so an older answer's fixed-text chip can no longer take the newest carrier's intent — the label-collision part of the "no binding" finding is closed. (Session 76, 2026-09-04: that binding now lives as `message.carrier` on `ChatMessage` — `web/lib/chat-message.ts` — rather than the `carriersRef` ref-Map PR #122 used; same behavior, see row #73.) The age-bound/TTL half is unchanged and still the owner's call.** |
| 181 | GDPR / trial — ✅ **BUILT (dormant)** | **An anonymous trial answer's CONTENT (verbatim question + answer + topic-disclosing table ids) is retained 2 years with no self-service erasure route, while its abuse bookkeeping is deleted at 90 days.** Verified by the session-58 GDPR lens: the 2-year purge DOES reach these rows (its WHERE keys on scope + age, never `user_id` — test-pinned), but `deleteMyQuestionHistory` binds `user_id` and an anonymous visitor has none. ADR 036 D4 applies exactly this "retention without purpose" reasoning to the bookkeeping and not to the content. Option A: redact `anonymous_trial` rows at the same 90-day cutoff (one extra scoped leg reusing `redactMatchingRows`). Option B: keep 2 years and write down why. Either is fine; the present state is the one that is neither. Written into [05-data-rules.md](05-data-rules.md) in session 58. **✅ BUILT 2026-07-26 (session 59): Option A — anonymous content is redacted at the SAME 90-day cutoff as its bookkeeping.** Shipped as a **two-cutoff single fragment**, not a fourth purge leg and not a change to who is in scope: `AUDIT_PURGE_WHERE` = `((account_scope and created_at < $1) or (anon_scope and created_at < $2))`. A separate leg was rejected because an anonymous row older than 2 years would match BOTH windows — double-counting the dry run and leaving "which leg redacted it" with two answers — and narrowing `AUDIT_SCOPE` was rejected because that fragment is also the SELF-SERVICE scope, so mutating it to fix a cutoff would move the wrong axis. Preview and apply are built from the same string, so the ⟨F2⟩ discipline holds by construction rather than by comment. **One constant, one meaning:** `ANONYMOUS_TRIAL_RETENTION_DAYS` lives in `retention.ts` and `trial-pot.ts` IMPORTS it — that direction, because billing may depend on answer and never the reverse (ADR 001's arrow). Both drift directions are named bugs: content outliving bookkeeping recreates this very row, bookkeeping outliving content leaves a visitor id + IP hash pointing at a redacted stub. **Keying invariant, written as a NEGATIVE because getting it wrong is silent:** the anonymous half must never be keyed through `trial_questions.audit_answer_id` — the bookkeeping DELETE removes those rows on the same clock, so a join-keyed redaction would orphan any content whose bookkeeping went first. Keyed on `audit_answers`' own scope + age, leg ORDER cannot matter, and it is verified that the existing code already keys that way. **The operator line now names both cutoffs and splits the dry-run count by window** — for an unattended cron that line is the only view of what happened, and a bare total says nothing about which of two clocks 21 months apart just bit. **R8 unaffected:** `verify-audit-rows.ts` already routes redacted rows to `redactionIntegrityReport` rather than reconstruction, so these appear as an increase in the redacted-row count, never as new divergences — the one real consequence is that anonymous rows lose reconstructability at 90 days instead of 2 years, which is the accepted trade for `user` rows already. **Mutations, re-measured across the WHOLE `tests/audit` suite after a review caught the first count being taken from one file only:** collapsing the anonymous window into the account window fails **2**; drifting the constant to 180 fails **4**. (The commit body first claimed 1 for the second — a Golden-Rule slip of exactly the kind the rule names: a number stated from a partial run. Corrected in the follow-up commit.) The window-equality test is labelled a GUARD, not a proof — `trial-pot.ts` imports the constant, so no mutation of the number alone can fail it; what it catches is someone reintroducing a literal. The same review also found a genuine BOUNDARY gap — the anonymous half's `created_at < $2` is a textually separate literal from the account half's, so the account leg's strict-`<` test could not catch it regressing to `<=`; there is now a test seeding a row AT `anonymousTrialCutoff` and one a second older. It also caught a test named "89 days" that seeded 88, and an ⟨F2⟩ test whose name overstated it (both paths derive the cutoff from the same call, so it can only catch them DISAGREEING) — both renamed to what they prove. ✅ **`GDPR_PURGE_APPLY=1` is now SET (2026-09-05, session 78)** — the monthly cron actually redacts, not just
reports ([#189](open-questions.md)); the first anonymous rows become purgeable **~2026-10-15** (go-live
2026-07-17 + 90). **The one open question, and the only reason to ask anything at all:** does the owner want anonymous trial questions kept as PRODUCT data — what people ask before signing up is the most valuable corpus this product has for [#169](open-questions.md)-style work? That is a business call, not a legal one. If yes, keep 2 years and write the reason down; if no or no answer, 90 days stands, because "retention without purpose" is exactly what ADR 036 D4 forbids. Asked in the PR body as a single line. |
| 185 | Trial / compensation | **Three connected facts about the trial refund, none of them dangerous today.** (a) A refund restores the *abuse counters* as well as the pot — both limit counts filter `and not refunded` — so any future throw path reachable after the LLM calls would become an unmetered free-LLM loop from one cookie and one IP. (b) It is nearly dead code: `respondToQuestion` catches everything and returns an internal refusal instead of throwing, so a visitor whose question dies on an Anthropic 529 is SERVED a refusal and **loses** a proefvraag. (c) If the pipeline throws while the pooler is full, the refund itself throws, is not retried, and masks the original error. | **All three recorded, none fixed.** (a) is latent-only — verified by tracing every post-LLM step (`respondToQuestion`'s outer catch, `persistOrFailClosed`'s nested catches, all three `maybeAlert*` "never throws" wrappers, `attachTrialAudit`'s fail-soft) and no attacker-shaped input reaches the refund. ~~Cheap hardening: drop `and not refunded` from the two COUNT queries so a refund returns the pot unit without returning the budget.~~ **DECLINED 2026-07-26, with reasoning, rather than shipped.** That change makes a visitor whose question died on an INFRASTRUCTURE failure lose one of their two free questions — a certain, immediate fairness cost — to close a hazard that is (a) latent, since `respondToQuestion` catches every attacker-shaped throw and no path reaches the refund, and (b) **already bounded in euros by the trial key's own hard spend cap**, which is the outer belt ADR 036 D3 put there for exactly this. Trading a certain cost against a bounded-and-unreachable one is the wrong direction. If the hazard ever becomes reachable, the better fix is a **refund CAP per visitor** (restore the pot always, restore the budget only for the first N) — it keeps the ordinary failure fair and still kills the loop. Recorded here so the next session does not re-derive it from the one-line suggestion. (b) is a product-honesty question for the owner, not a bug: ADR 036 chose "every served response consumes the question" deliberately, but an infrastructure 529 is arguably not a served response. (c) bounded at one question. |
| 186 | Trial / capacity — ✅ **MEASURED + BUILT** | **Every anonymous homepage GET costs 1-2 UNCACHED Postgres queries against the 15-session ceiling, with no auth gate, no billing gate, no cache and no rate limit** — the trial gate reads the pot per request by design (so a refill re-opens the trial without a deploy) plus a second count when a `cdc_trial` cookie is present. Ranked mitigations: (1) measure (`pg_stat_activity` via the management API, which bypasses the pooler, quiet hour vs deploy) — risk 0; (2) a 15-30 s in-process cache on the POT READ ONLY, which preserves the no-deploy refill to within the TTL and cannot over-serve because the atomic take is the real gate; (3) `s-maxage` on `/llms.txt` so the edge serves it; (4) the Vercel Firewall rate rule ADR 036 D2 already names as an owner option — **now load-bearing rather than optional, and it must target `POST /`**: Server Actions POST to the page path, so the RUNBOOK's previously-suggested `/api/` rule would have spent Hobby's single free rule on two already-authenticated routes (corrected in the RUNBOOK, session 58B). The homepage itself must NOT be edge-cached (cookie-dependent auth + per-visitor gate). **✅ MEASURED 2026-07-26 (session 59), then BUILT.** Mitigation (1) ran first, as the row asked, and the instrument that answered it was **`pg_stat_statements`, not `pg_stat_activity`** — the latter is a snapshot and shows only what is connected right now, while the former had been accumulating since 2026-07-02 07:28 and never reset. **The numbers:** `select remaining_questions, cap from trial_pot_config` = **143 calls**, `stats_since` **2026-07-17 17:27** (the trial go-live, session 52) ⇒ ~8.2 days ⇒ **~17 anonymous landing renders/day**, mean **0.443 ms** — against **5** per-visitor counts and **2** questions ever served (`trial_questions` = 2 rows, pot 23/25). So the shape the row describes is real and the volume today is trivial; this ships as headroom, not as a fix for a live pressure. **The finding that changed the design:** the cost is not the 0.44 ms query, it is the pooler SESSION the query forces the instance to open. Sampled at a quiet hour (22:54 Amsterdam), a single anonymous GET left a Supavisor backend **idle for 174 s and counting**, and **4 of the 15 session slots were held (72-174 s) from roughly two page views** — because node-pg's 10 s `idleTimeoutMillis` does not fire while a Fluid Compute instance is frozen. Hence the cache is aimed at sessions, not milliseconds. ⚠ **Stated against the state that actually ships (#186 + [#184](open-questions.md) together): a STEADY-STATE anonymous render costs ONE query, cookie or no cookie, down from 1-2** — the render that refreshes the pot costs two, at most once per TTL per instance rather than once per visitor. #186 alone would have made a cookie-less drive-by cost **zero** queries and need no session at all; #184 spends that back to buy its honesty fix, deliberately — see that row. The pot read itself drops from every-GET to at most once per TTL per instance either way, which is the part that does not depend on #184. Also verified in passing: direct-Postgres `max_connections` is **60** (the binding ceiling is Supavisor's 15, not Postgres's), and the RUNBOOK's diagnosis recipe `application_name like 'Supavisor%'` is **correct** — app sessions do report exactly `Supavisor`. **NOT measured: the deploy-window leg**, because an autonomous session does not merge and so produces no deploy; the 2026-07-25 incident remains the only evidence for that arm. **Built:** `TRIAL_POT_TTL_MS = 20_000` in `web/lib/trial.ts` with single-flight coalescing (the `ontdek.ts`/`llms-txt.ts` pattern), **pot read only** — the per-visitor count stays live on every request, because sharing it across requests on a reused instance would hand one visitor another's budget. Two deliberate divergences from the ontdek precedent, both pinned by tests: a failed read is **never cached** (a stale 'open' costs nothing since the atomic take is the real gate, while a stale 'unavailable' costs a visitor the whole trial), and an **expired** entry is never resurrected on failure — ontdek's stale-over-nothing is right for charts and wrong for an invitation to type into a backend we just observed to be down. `resetTrialPotCache()` is the test seam; `trialpot:set` now warns the operator that a reload may still show the old state. **Five mutations run, each mapped one-to-one to a named test** (TTL=0 → 2 fail; no single-flight → 1; no expiry check → 2; stale-over-nothing → 1); the remaining cases pass on revert by construction and are labelled guards, not proofs — including one tautology this found and fixed, where the expiry test advanced the clock BY the constant and so could not fail on its magnitude (a range assertion now pins 15-30 s). **Next lever if traffic grows, deliberately NOT built:** a bounded per-`ip_hash` TTL cache for the [#184](open-questions.md) count — held back because it is per-visitor-class data and a keying bug there is a cross-visitor leak, which is not worth it at 17 GETs/day. |
| 188 | Trial / capacity (#173 family) | **Waiting for the trial's global advisory lock PINS one of the 15 pooler sessions for the whole wait.** `takeTrialQuestion` acquires a pool client, opens a transaction, and takes `pg_advisory_xact_lock` over ONE constant key as its first statement (`src/billing/trial-pot.ts:77`) — so a burst of anonymous submits becomes session-OCCUPANCY time on the scarce resource, not just queued work. Rejections are not cheap either: the per-IP and per-visitor checks run INSIDE the locked transaction, so traffic the limiter correctly refuses still pays the full session-plus-lock cost, and can starve the PAID product on the same instances. Measured query budget from code: **1 pooled query per warm anonymous landing render** (~2 with a cookie, ~33-42 cold/expired) and **~25-50 per served trial question**, 8 under the lock. found while building this), toggle as a `radiogroup`, `schemaVersion` guard in `chart.tsx`, the optional-v1-field rule written into ADR 014, `known-divergences.ts` header corrected. Tests render the REAL svg in jsdom (`initialDimension` + no ResizeObserver stub) — 27 new pins, web suite 529/529. **Decisions taken as defaults (veto by exception):** presentation preferences per-device only (none persisted yet — step 1 has no user-adjustable state); exports bake in whatever is on screen (#46(c) → bake); palette = validated neutral set, house colours only after the same check; chat and Ontdek both get step 1 (it is the shared ChartView). **Step 2 BUILT same session:** a Grafiek/Tabel switch (`tablist`/`tab`, arrow keys) on every `ChartView`; `tableModel` lays a line chart out as period × series and a comparison as one row per region under its period, every cell the point's own `formattedValue` (+ `*`) bound via `data-label-for`, a null cell as `— (CBS reason)`; a comparison with more than 15 series OPENS on the table (the idea-bank rule); the image download is offered in chart view only. **Deviation from the brief, taken as a default:** no third "CSV" entry in the download menu — WP21's "Download als CSV" button already sits directly under every chat answer (charted or not), and Ontdek has no answer to export; a duplicate entry adds nothing. **Step 3 BUILT (session 70, 2026-09-02) on branch `feat/197-3-comparison-chips`, MERGED 2026-09-03 (session 71, squash `83f790e`, PR #118, CI run 33699880673 gate + deploy green) once the owner.s `CLARIFY_CLICK_ENABLED` smoke test passed in production** (rows 261/262; session 70 had found zero chip-click takes ever. The RUNBOOK.s "bare Utrecht" input was wrong — the one-word question is refused as smalltalk (row 260, refunded); the flagship `Hoeveel inwoners had Utrecht in 2024?` clarifies with two chips — corrected in `2d27175`). Two comparison generators (`compareRegion`: answered regions + `NL01`, or `NL01` + the G4; `comparePeriod`: the same period one year earlier as the `difference` derivation) placed ahead of the region variant (skipped once a comparison surfaced); each chip carries a dry-run-proven `ClickOption` on a present-only `AnswerResponse.pending` in the WP26c chip-carrier shape (`rescueOnly`), taken through the zero-LLM `templateOnly` path as a NEW validated result (R6), 20 credits (brief decision 4's leaning), real audit row. Only with `CLARIFY_CLICK_ENABLED` on; off is byte-identical. `isRescuePending` widened to 1..4 label-bound chips (ADR 024 addendum). **Deviations taken as defaults:** no "Sinds 2008" chip (no honest floor without db access — the trend chip covers a proven window); carrier-bound labels (comparison AND the WP26c rescue chip) are dropped on thread resume because no pending is restored there (ADR 033 ⟨A6⟩) — restoring the carrier on resume is a possible follow-up — so LIVE shows all chips and a RESUMED thread shows only the question-shaped ones, deliberately (do not "fix" it back). Reachability under cap 3: on a regional single answer adjacent + trend + "Vergelijk met Nederland" fill the three slots, so "Vergelijk met <vorig jaar>" surfaces only on national-only measures (CPI) or when adjacent/trend fail; cap 4 under the flag was considered, left for usage data. The comparisons start from the RESOLVED intent, so a B-region-defaulted national answer (once `ANSWER_FIRST_ENABLED` is on) still gets its G4 chip with explicit regions. Cost: up to +2 dry-runs per flag-on answer turn (#195). **Two sessions received the session-70 kickoff in the same tree (the 25-07 shape again); the second stopped building, moved to a worktree and became the independent reviewer — its findings are folded in here: the resolved-intent source, the takeability gate (an `onboarded:` key would have minted a chip the click-time validator strips → a paid LLM merge; now `isClickTakeableIntent` gates every candidate, pinned), the forgery pins, the replay parity pin, the reachability note, and a measured cost pin in `query-count.test.ts` (27/27 regional, 39/39 national, 12→18 national-only; #195).** **Follow-ups recorded, NOT built:** (i) the `templateOnly` comparison always closes with "Nederland had de hoogste waarde." on a count measure (renderComparison's pre-registered max) — true but vacuous; suppressing it when a national row is present changes the deterministic template for every stored multi-region-with-NL row = an R8 re-derivation divergence → needs a `known-divergences.ts` pin + `audit:verify` (#133 policy) before it may ship; (ii) the dashboard history titles a taken comparison under the ORIGINAL question (the reply-row convention, ADR 024 addendum) — "title = reply_text on reply rows" is a possible UI tweak; (iii) restoring the chip carrier on thread resume. **Ideas 6 and 8 BUILT (session 79, 2026-09-05, direct push to `main` pending final verification):** idea 6 (series legend — one button per series with click-to-hide, any chart kind with 2+ series: line or bar/comparison, gated only on `seriesMeta.length > 1` with no `spec.kind` check) and idea 8 (small multiples — one mini line chart per series, line charts with 2+ series only — `smallMultiplesAvailable` additionally requires `spec.kind === 'line'` — manual toggle, off by default, interacts with idea 6: hidden series have no panel). Ideas 4, 5, 7 unscheduled. **#73 v2 (session 72, 2026-09-03; PR #122 MERGED + LIVE 2026-09-03, session 75, owner-approved in chat, squash `4fd6ea5`): the comparison carrier became the GENERAL chip carrier — every takeable follow-up chip under an answer now rides it (ADR 029 v2 as-built note); follow-up (iii) is unchanged and now also covers the question-shaped chips' takes on resume.** **Idea 4 (trend headline) BUILT + fixed + re-reviewed + MERGED, sessions 80-81 (2026-09-05/06, `main` `a1e16e9`, owner-approved merge in-chat).** Designed ([session-briefs/2026-09-05-chart-ideas-4-6-8-design.md](session-briefs/2026-09-05-chart-ideas-4-6-8-design.md) §3), planned ([superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-chart-trend-headline.md](superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-chart-trend-headline.md)), all 3 tasks built via `subagent-driven-development`: `renderTrendHeadline` formatter (`b733469`), `ChartAttribution.trendHeadline` field + schema + `buildChartSpec` wiring (`5c8c9c1`), rendered in `chart.tsx` (`7944fd7`). **The final whole-branch review (opus) found 2 Critical + 2 Important issues, all real and reproduced against the actual code, none hypothetical** — fixed in one round (`af21ab8`), independently re-reviewed (a second opus pass checking the fix against `deriveDirection`/`monotonic` directly, not just trusting the fix report) and confirmed correct with no new issues beyond doc gaps (this row). The shipped behavior is deliberately narrower than first built: (1) suppressed entirely on multi-region charts — `deriveDirection` has no region guard, so a multi-region chart's derivation would otherwise silently diff across DIFFERENT regions (a real, reproduced false trend claim); the underlying gap in `deriveDirection` is pre-existing on `main` (not introduced by this branch) and tracked as [#203](open-questions.md), adjacent to [#100](open-questions.md); (2) suppressed entirely for `direction: 'flat'` + non-monotonic (no honest one-line phrasing exists for "rose then fell back to its start value"); (3) the "gestaag" (steadily) claim now needs ≥3 source points — a 2-point series is trivially monotonic, the same hazard class [#100](open-questions.md) already tracks for the LLM prompt's reading of `monotonic`, closed here for this separate deterministic template. Also fixed: every historical chart row with a `direction` derivation would otherwise fail R8 reconstruction once this shipped (rebuilding an unchanged row now yields a field the stored row never had) — `reconstruct.ts` carries a new narrow, named, field-scoped tolerance (full mechanism: ADR 014 as-built notes; not user-facing — `threads/replay.ts` serves stored charts verbatim). Final measured state on the branch: root+web typecheck clean, backend 119 files / 1814 tests, benchmark GATE PASS (14/14+6/6+0 fabricated), web 51 files / 610 tests, real `next build` clean. Owner chose to skip the #89 `AnswerProof` info-icon for v1 (would need a `proof` prop threaded through `dock-visuals.ts`/`visual-dock.tsx`/`chart-toggle.tsx`, none of which carry one today) — the headline ships as plain text only. **MERGED (`a1e16e9`) — the owner reviewed and merged in-chat once back; `deploy` on this commit is pending the same Route B Vercel-secrets gap as every push since session 79 (owner's own `gh secret set` commands), not specific to this change.** |
| 198 | WP26 B / intent calibration | **Answer-first (mechanism B) fires, but on a live Haiku parse a period-less question lands under the R7 answer threshold and becomes a one-chip confirmation instead of a direct answer — measured on the go-live canary, 2026-09-03 (session 71).** "Hoeveel inwoners telde Amsterdam?": row 265 (05:42Z, flag not yet deployed) clarified on the PERIOD axis ("Voor welke periode…"); row 266 (06:40Z, flag live) no longer asks for a period — B-period did its job — but the single candidate carries `confidence: 0.85` with the reading "bevolking van Amsterdam (gemeente) zonder specifieke periode genoemd", i.e. the parser books the MISSING PERIOD as "mild doubt" (prompt rule: "0.6–0.85 plausible with mild doubt"), so R7 rule 3 (below-threshold single reading, `answerThreshold 0.9`) produced "Bedoel je …?" with ONE click chip (10 credits, the chip 20). Not a code bug: ADR 024 explicitly keeps "rule-3 below-threshold single reading" as a clarification; the hermetic answer-first pin (`answer-first-reply-turn.test.ts`) uses a fixture parse at 0.95, so it could not see this. The paid dead-end is gone (the chip works, zero-LLM), but the "answers directly" promise of B only holds when the parser is confident about everything EXCEPT the period. | **Recorded, NOT fixed (prompt bytes + real spend = owner-supervised). Two remedies, default first:** (1) **prompt rule** next to the existing "do not lower confidence merely because the user did not spell out the technical definition": *do not lower confidence merely because no period (or no place) is named — code fills or asks for those axes*; requires re-recording the intent fixtures + `intent:eval` at or above the current calibration (real spend, RUNBOOK) and a period-less case in the labelled set — the honest fix, because the doubt is not real doubt; (2) **code-side**: when `answerFirstEnabled` and the single candidate's only unusual trait is `period.kind = 'none'`, treat confidence ≥ 0.8 as the threshold for that candidate — cheaper, but it second-guesses the parser's number and must be pinned + reviewed against R7. Watch item meanwhile (RUNBOOK step 5): how often rows with `periodDefaulted`/`regionDefaulted` appear versus rule-3 confirmations on period-less/place-less questions. **OPTION 1 CHOSEN (owner "1" in chat, 2026-09-03, session 71) — ATTEMPTED, MEASURED, REVERTED the same session; back to the owner with numbers.** Three wordings of the rule were tried as intent prompt v7, each with the four fixture sets re-recorded live (intent/followup/clarify/onboarding-delivery all embed the intent system prompt) and the 3-repeat eval: (1) a separate two-sentence bullet → "Hoeveel inwoners telde Amsterdam?" 0.92 (was 0.85) and the labelled set clean, BUT the unrelated open-range case `os-v02` flipped `difference`→`none`; (2) one clause on the confidence bullet ("A missing period or place is NOT doubt…") → os-v02 stable, Amsterdam 0.92, final live eval 74/74 zero flips, calibration floor 0.92 — BUT the **onboarding-delivery** parse for "Hoeveel mensen zaten er in 2023 in de bijstand?" (the #124 measurement, an onboarded table with 18 same-tagged measures) dropped from 0.92 to **0.88 in three of three runs** (note: "specifieke subcategorieën … zijn ook beschikbaar"), turning the pinned `delivered` into `unanswerable`; (3) the existing style ("Do NOT lower confidence merely because no period or no place is named…") → delivery parse **0.85**, worse. Conclusion: any sentence about not-lowering-confidence-for-a-missing-axis makes Haiku MORE hesitant on the onboarded sibling-measure case — the two goals fight in the prompt. Reverted to v6 (`git checkout` of prompt, fixtures, reports, labelled set); nothing shipped. Spend ≈ $10 in Haiku tokens. **Two labelled cases worth keeping for the next attempt** (not committed, they need fixtures): `a-amsterdam-geen-periode` "Hoeveel inwoners telde Amsterdam?" → `clarification` with `axesInclude: ["period"]` (the intent layer clarifies on period when confident; a rule-3 `measure` clarification means the parser hedged); `a-2024-geen-plaats` "Hoeveel inwoners waren er in 2024?" → `intent` with `checks.regions: ["NL01"]` (the resolver maps regions null to the national row; the query layer decides). **Recommendation now: option 2 (code-side)** — in the intent resolver, when `answerFirstEnabled` and the single candidate has `period.kind = 'none'` or `regions = null`, use 0.8 instead of 0.9 as the answer threshold for THAT candidate only, pinned with the two cases above at their recorded live confidences (0.85 / 0.95); it changes no prompt bytes, no fixtures, and cannot touch the onboarded-delivery path (a named period). **Owner (in chat, 2026-09-03, after a plain-language explanation): "later" — PARKED. Not blocking: users get the confirmation chip and one click answers. Pick up in a later session only on the owner's go.** |
| 200 | Ingestion / test-infra | **Discovered 2026-09-04, session 76 (autonomous Dependabot-verification legwork on PR #124): the hermetic LLM replay-fixture system is fragile to a NON-behavioral `zod` bump.** `src/answer/llm/client.ts`'s `requestHash()` hashes the full `LlmRequest`, including `request.jsonSchema` built via `z.toJSONSchema()` at four call sites (`src/answer/intent/schema.ts`, `src/catalog/rerank-schema.ts`, `src/ingestion/onboarding-fit.ts`, `src/answer/compose/semantic-check.ts`). Every hermetic test replays a committed fixture keyed by this hash. Bumping `zod` 4.4.3→4.5.4 (PR #124, an otherwise-clean minor bump — zod's own changelog documents `toJSONSchema()` output-SHAPE fixes, e.g. zod#6497) changed that serialized output enough to change the hash, so all four call sites' fixtures stopped matching: 49/1780 backend tests failed, benchmark refusal/clarify gate went 0/6 (required 6/6), reproduced identically on CI (two independent runs) AND locally. **Confirmed the mechanism, not just hypothesized it:** re-running the benchmark with `zod` alone pinned back to 4.4.3 (all 4 other PR #124 bumps left at their new versions) restored GATE VERDICT: PASS, 6/6/6/6/0 — proof by elimination, not guesswork. web/'s own suite (a separate `package.json`, unaffected by this PR, still on zod ^4.4.3) stayed 100% green throughout on identical application code, corroborating the isolation. **Not fixed here** (would mean touching the fixture-recording system or re-recording fixtures, both explicitly owner-supervised — "no fixture-herrecording" per session-76's own binding constraints). PR #124 itself carries the full write-up + the bisection proof as PR comments; not merged. | **Open — architectural fragility, not urgent** (no zod bump is imminent beyond this one blocked PR, and this doesn't affect production — only the hermetic test/fixture-replay harness). Two possible directions if picked up later, neither attempted: (a) make `requestHash()` more tolerant — hash a stable, semantically-normalized projection of the JSON Schema rather than its raw serialized bytes, so a library's output-formatting change (same schema, different bytes) doesn't invalidate every fixture; (b) accept the coupling as-is but add a CI check that fails fast and NAMES the cause (e.g. "a captured jsonSchema literal changed — check zod's version, not the request contract") instead of the current generic "no recorded fixture" message, which cost real diagnosis time this session. Whoever revisits: read PR #124's comments first, the bisection proof is already done. **Direction (b) BUILT** (branch `fix/200-fixture-hash-diagnostic`, autonomous session, PR open per #118(b) — not merged): `ReplayLlmClient.complete()` in `src/answer/llm/client.ts` now, on a hash miss, scans `fixturesDir` for another fixture whose request is `stableStringify`-identical with `jsonSchema` excluded from the comparison — a near-miss on every field except `jsonSchema` — and if found, appends a paragraph to the thrown error naming that fixture's own hash and truncated question, stating plainly (not asserting certainty) that this pattern usually means a schema-serialization library like zod's `toJSONSchema()` changed its output shape in a dependency bump rather than the prompt/schema/model/registry actually changing, and pointing at this row (#200) so a future reader jumps straight to the bisection write-up instead of re-diagnosing from scratch; when no near-miss exists the thrown message is unchanged byte-for-byte from before (pinned by a dedicated new regression test), and `requestHash()`/`stableStringify()`/`RecordingLlmClient`/the four `jsonSchema`-building call sites were not touched, so this carries zero prompt-byte risk. **Direction (a) is still open and not attempted** — hashing a semantically-normalized JSON Schema projection instead of raw bytes remains a possible future follow-up if near-miss diagnosis proves insufficient. **Direction (c) BUILT, session 80 (2026-09-05): held zod out of the automated bump entirely, root-cause rather than routed around.** `.github/dependabot.yml`'s `npm-all` group now `ignore`s zod (`c6fa764`), mirroring the 2026-07-17 TypeScript-major hold (`eec3973`) — no future weekly PR will re-propose this same regression. The blocked PR (renumbered #2 after Route B's repo recreation reset Dependabot's numbering) is superseded: Dependabot itself closed #2 within minutes of the ignore rule landing and opened PR #4 with the same 4 safe packages this session had independently bumped by hand in a scratch worktree (byte-identical target versions, confirmed) — zod excluded, staying pinned at `^4.4.3`. PR #4's own changelogs were checked against actual call sites (no beta-namespace Anthropic SDK usage in `src/answer/llm/`; no `cryptogram`/`three_d_secure`/`PaymentAttemptRecord`/V2RuntimeSchema Stripe usage anywhere in `src/` — this codebase's only Stripe calls are `checkout.sessions.create` and `Stripe.webhooks.constructEvent`) with no relevant breaking change found. Direction (a) remains the only unattempted direction, now lower-priority since (c) prevents the recurring nuisance even without it. |
| 201 | Product / architecture | **Owner idea (2026-09-05, session 79): let people connect their own Google Sheets as a data source in chat** — sharing a sheet via a Google-account connection, the owner's own framing. **Owner immediately flagged the real blocker himself: this needs user accounts + Google OAuth, neither of which exists yet, and he does not want to build auth now** — recorded as-is, not decided further this session. | **Recorded as a future roadmap item, NOT scheduled, NOT the same shape as WP30c.** WP30c ([#123](open-questions.md)) is about a second OFFICIAL statistics source (bulk-ingested, registry-driven, ADR 030's narrow waist); a user's own Google Sheet is a different category entirely — unofficial, per-user, live/mutable content. Real tension with the product principles this needs to resolve before design: **(a)** the public claim ("every number traceable to an official CBS cell") and principle (a) (LLM never interprets raw data) would need a distinct, clearly-labelled trust tier for user-supplied data, analogous to how [ADR 032](decisions/032-websearch-augmentation.md) keeps websearch results in their own separately-attributed section rather than the validated CBS body; **(b)** principle (b) ("bulk-ingested... never queried live") assumes curated, pre-vetted official sources — a user's sheet can change anytime and isn't vetted, so it likely needs its own live-read-or-resync path architecturally separate from the WP30 waist, not an extension of it. **Blocked on:** user accounts/auth (owner-deferred), Google OAuth + sheet-sharing flow, the new trust/attribution model above. Needs its own brainstorm + ADR once auth is real — not a WP30c variant. **Design drafted 2026-09-06 (session 84) alongside [#202](open-questions.md):** [session-briefs/2026-09-06-chat-with-data-design.md](session-briefs/2026-09-06-chat-with-data-design.md) covers file/link uploads end to end but keeps Google Sheets itself OUT of scope — still blocked on OAuth data-scope grants + refresh-token storage, which do not exist (accounts themselves now exist, ADR 028 — the "no accounts" framing above was shorthand for the missing Sheets-scope grant, not literally no auth). **Owner read-back complete 2026-09-06 (same session) — all 9 §8 decisions resolved, recorded in the doc. Adversarially reviewed (7 lenses), then PROMOTED to accepted [ADR 037](decisions/037-user-data-attachments.md) same session, and its entire backend built across 5 slices (WP202a — see [docs/08-build-plan.md](08-build-plan.md)), all pushed to `main`, all green.** **Session 85 (2026-09-06/07) finished the backend AND built the entire UI** — Server Actions, thread-kind dispatch, `UserChartView`, `DatasetChat`, and the upload button are all wired end to end (`docs/08-build-plan.md`'s WP202a section, 6 more commits, all green). Not yet live: no `ATTACHMENTS_ENABLED` flag, migrations 026/027 still file-only (never applied to any real database). Google Sheets itself remains explicitly out of scope either way — this row stays open until Sheets-scope OAuth is designed. |
