this row (a real subscription tier) is still the actual blocker to closing the loop, exactly as before. **Addendum, session 101 continued (2026-09-13): the brainstorm this row asked for happened.** Design doc (owner-approved): [session-briefs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-13-pro-subscription-tier-design.md](session-briefs/2026-09-13-pro-subscription-tier-design.md) — scope = Live embeds + a monthly credit allowance (not unlimited chat); price = €19.99/month, 1000 credits/period; the allowance resets, unused lost (a scoped, deliberate exception to "credits never expire," isolated in its own ledger table specifically so the exception can never leak into permanent credits); cancellation/failed-payment access continues to `current_period_end`; ships flag-gated off (`PRO_SUBSCRIPTIONS_ENABLED`), same posture as `EMBED_TOKEN_SECRET`. 13-task implementation plan written: [superpowers/plans/2026-09-13-pro-subscription-tier.md](superpowers/plans/2026-09-13-pro-subscription-tier.md). **Addendum, 2026-09-14 — ✅ built, flag-gated off:** the design (owner-approved 2026-09-13) is fully implemented per its 13-task plan (branch `worktree-pro-subscription-tier`, PR #22, not yet merged into `main`): a fully isolated `pro_subscriptions` + `pro_bucket_ledger` schema (migration 030, zero changes to `credit_transactions`), a bucket-first `splitDebit` spend path wired into every existing debit function (byte-identical for non-Pro users, the full pre-existing test suite pins this), `buildProSubscriptionCheckoutParams` + `customer.subscription.*`/`invoice.paid` webhook handlers that grant and rotate the monthly allowance, and the embed dialog's Upgrade button now wired to a real Checkout session (`startProSubscriptionCheckout`, `web/app/embed-actions.ts`). `hasProPlan` now checks the `PRO_ACCOUNT_EMAILS` allowlist OR a real `pro_subscriptions` row — the allowlist stays as the owner's own testing override, exactly as the session-93 addendum above already anticipated. ADR [006](decisions/006-auth-billing-seams.md) (alternative 3)/[020](decisions/020-credit-ledger-and-billing-gate.md) (decision 8) both now carry the revision notes this row long said they'd need; [09-pricing.md](09-pricing.md) has the €19.99/month, 1000-credits-resetting-unused Pro tier alongside the existing tables. **✅ PR #22 MERGED 2026-09-14 (`53c7703`), CI green, deployed, smoke check passed.** ⚠ Merged before migration 030 — exactly the total-outage risk this row already warned about — caught during the same session's wrap-up and fixed within ~30 minutes (migration applied, RLS verified; full account in [RUNBOOK.md](RUNBOOK.md)'s Pro subscription go-live section and [lessons-learned.md](lessons-learned.md)). **Still owner-supervised before this can charge anyone for real:** create the real Stripe Price object, set `STRIPE_PRO_PRICE_ID`, subscribe the webhook destination to `customer.subscription.*`/`invoice.paid`, and only then set `PRO_SUBSCRIPTIONS_ENABLED=1` — steps 4-8 of [RUNBOOK.md](RUNBOOK.md)'s Pro subscription go-live section. This row stays open (not archived) until that flag is actually flipped for a real subscriber; the mechanism itself is done and live (dormant). **Readiness checked 2026-09-14:** `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY`/`STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET` already set (pre-existing); `STRIPE_PRO_PRICE_ID`/`PRO_SUBSCRIPTIONS_ENABLED` confirmed not set; the checkout/webhook code read directly and confirmed real, no stubs. Only the owner's own Stripe-dashboard + env-var steps remain — see RUNBOOK's readiness-check note. A real, separate display-only gap found during this build's own review is tracked at [#246](open-questions.md) — it does not block this row. |

| 211 | Product / UX / architecture | **Sub-project 2 of [#210](open-questions.md): "chat interaction polish"** — the feel of using the chat (streaming responses, input affordances, overall flow), not yet designed. A groundwork research brief was produced session 87 (2026-09-07, read-only investigation, no code/docs changed by it) covering: whether real token-by-token streaming is architecturally feasible (yes for the phrasing-only LLM call, since numbers are validated before that call runs — but the pipeline validates the LLM's FULL output *after* generation and can silently retry/fall back to a different body, a real tension between "stream what's generating" and "only show what's validated" that needs an owner decision, not a workaround); what streaming would require technically (no `ai`/`@ai-sdk/*` package installed yet; a Route Handler + SSE sidesteps Server Action streaming quirks; the billing gate's debit-before/compensate-after logic needs the pipeline's *final* outcome, which isn't known mid-stream); other friction points observed (clarification-chip legibility, fill-don't-send follow-up chips, inconsistent attachment-row affordance states, a single generic busy line with no stage feedback); and 3 concrete approach options (Route Handler+SSE stream the phrasing call; Server Action + AI SDK streamable value; no architecture change, just a staged busy-state UI as the smallest safe win). | **Not decided, not scheduled.** Needs its own brainstorming round with the owner before any build — the validation-timing tension above is the one thing that must be resolved as a real product decision, not assumed. **Addendum (session 87, later same day):** re-examined the brief's own "no architecture change" option 3 (staged busy-state text: "Zoekt cijfers op" → "Valideert" → "Formuleert antwoord") before building it and found it doesn't actually hold up — the client has no real signal for which pipeline stage is active (one awaited Server Action, no intermediate events), so "staged" text would have to be fake/time-based, which conflicts with the product's own "never fake it" principle (the exact reasoning the disabled-button "Binnenkort beschikbaar" chips already follow). Shipped: loading skeletons in the chat answer area, the chart panel (only when already open from an earlier turn), and thread switching; plus a drag-to-resize chart panel (width does NOT persist across visits — a real bug in `react-resizable-panels@4.12.4`'s own layout-restore path, traced and confirmed, not worth chasing further right now). | 212 | Product / UX / architecture | **Sub-project 3 of [#210](open-questions.md): "conversational chart editing for CBS charts"** — adjusting an already-shown official-CBS chart by chatting (presentation only; filtering within already-fetched data; asking for different/additional data). Owner decision (session 87) pulled this into scope, narrowing `docs/03-mvp-scope.md`'s "interactive chart studio" non-goal (the full drag-and-drop studio authoring tool stays Phase 2–3; this narrower slice does not). A groundwork research brief was produced session 87 (read-only, no code/docs changed by it), reading `docs/05-data-rules.md` in full plus ADR [037](decisions/037-user-data-attachments.md)'s `ChartInstruction`/`execute.ts` precedent for user-uploaded data: the *mechanism* (LLM emits a closed-vocabulary instruction, deterministic code validates + executes it) transfers, but the *data scope* doesn't — CBS `ValidatedResult.cells` only ever contains the narrow cell set one specific intent asked for (no "full table" the way an uploaded dataset has), so a CBS-specific vocabulary must enumerate only what's already in a given result (regions/periods/dims present) and hard-refuse anything else to a new question. Invariant risk assessed per edit tier: presentation-only and in-hand-filtering are low-risk if built ADR-037-style (LLM never touches numbers, only selects from a closed enum reused verbatim, `resultId`/`formattedValue`/provisional marking preserved not recomputed); "ask for different data" is likely already-working via the existing follow-up/merged-intent mechanism (ADR 021) and may need no backend work at all, just a UX affordance. 3 concrete approach options for tiers 1+2 given (reuse ADR 037's exact machinery parameterized per-result; deterministic UI controls with chat routing to them, no new LLM instruction schema at all; a thin CBS-specific instruction schema scoped to `periodSubrange`/`regionSubset`/`kind` compatibility, living in `src/chart/` not `src/attachments/` to keep the two trust tiers separate). | **Not decided, not scheduled.** Needs its own brainstorming round with the owner — this sits closest to R1/R9/R10 of anything in the redesign effort, so the design round should re-verify the brief's invariant-risk read rather than build straight from it. **Tier-3 claim empirically confirmed (session 87, later same day, live on production):** asked a real follow-up for a different region ("En hoeveel inwoners had Utrecht in 2024?" after a Rotterdam answer) — the existing follow-up/clarification mechanism handled it correctly with zero new code: the Rotterdam answer stayed in place, a genuine PV-vs-gemeente disambiguation fired honestly, and the resolved Utrecht answer appended as its own StatCard with its own attribution/audit trail and fresh suggestion chips. Confirms the brief's read: tier 3 needs no backend work, only (if anything) a UX affordance to make it feel like "editing" rather than "a new answer below." **Owner feedback, session 88 (2026-09-08, given explicitly in passing — "doesn't mean you need to work on it now"):** wants users to be able to add a text annotation/indicator at a specific point on a chart, and leans toward this being a direct point-click-and-write interaction rather than something routed through chat. Worth weighing against this row's existing tier framing at the actual brainstorm — a click-to-annotate affordance is closer to tier 1 (presentation) than a chat-driven instruction, and may sit outside the `ChartInstruction`/closed-vocabulary mechanism entirely (a free-text user annotation is not a validated CBS number and would need its own honesty framing — never rendered as if it were part of the official data). **Architecture panel run, session 88 (2026-09-08, later same day)** — an ultracode 4-angle design panel + synthesis (5 agents, ~880k tokens), each angle re-reading `docs/05-data-rules.md`/ADR 037/the actual current chart code from scratch rather than trusting this row's own summary. Converged decision: **no LLM instruction schema in v1** — a deterministic view-state reducer (`src/chart/view.ts`, pure, no React) driven by direct on-screen controls (line/bar/table switch, period-range zoom, series hide/highlight, click-to-annotate notes anchored by `resultId`, notes rendered structurally OUTSIDE `ChartView`'s root so no R6 token-scan exemption is ever needed) — matching the owner's own click-first steer above and needing zero prompt bytes/LLM cost/DB schema for phases 1-3. A chat-routed producer (thin schema mirroring ADR 037's *shape*, not its code) is designed but explicitly deferred to a CONDITIONAL later phase, built only if usage evidence shows demand — not scheduled on spec alone. A map view is explicitly gated behind a NEW prerequisite (a "region-set" query capability the product doesn't have yet — today's CBS results only ever contain the 2-5 regions one question named, so a map of them would visually claim "no data" about everywhere else, an honesty problem, not an engineering one) — not a v1-adjacent option. Full synthesis (architecture, phased plan, per-piece R1/R9/R10 compliance table, 10 explicitly-rejected alternatives, 8 open owner decisions lettered A-H) published as a reviewable artifact: https://claude.ai/code/artifact/91b16e9d-d5aa-4223-a6cf-ff3a7001c938 (also ran the CLAUDE.md convention: this whole plan is itself the worked example of "cheapest mechanism first"). **Owner response so far:** "Great plan on most items so far" (not a line-by-line sign-off on A-H) followed by a request to dispatch an autonomous session to build what's safe — see the session-89 kickoff / the "Continue" session's own brief for exactly what got greenlit and on what basis. Still **not a formal owner decision on A-H** — whoever picks this row up next should check whether the autonomous session's build notes recorded explicit resolutions for each, or whether they remain open. **Autonomous session build, 2026-09-08 (branch `feat/chart-editing-p1`, per the [#118](open-questions.md) autonomous-session git-workflow rule — feature branch, PR, no direct push to `main`):** built Phases 1-3 of the architecture panel's plan as 6 feature/fix commit pairs, each independently reviewed: (1) `2720197` — a pure `chartViewReducer` + `windowSpec` projection (`web/lib/chart-view-state.ts`), no React, no `src/chart/` change; (2) `a297166` — consolidated `chart.tsx`'s presentation `useState`s into that reducer, plus a same-session fix `5a66d41` restoring the pre-reducer behavior of preserving a user's chosen chart form across a spec swap (the naive consolidation had silently reset it); (3) `89ac67f` — the three-way Lijn/Staaf/Tabel form switch with the multi-region-line guard (`lineFormAllowed`), which during self-review also picked up a same-instance-spec-swap honesty-guard gap (a disallowed multi-region spec could otherwise inherit a stale `'line'` form) before the commit landed, plus a follow-up fix `651b97c` for value labels not rendering after a cross-form switch; (4) `ed74af5` — Vanaf/Tot period-range zoom with an on-screen and export disclosure sentence, plus a follow-up fix `a66ba24` so the small-multiples view honors the same zoom (it was still reading the unwindowed spec); (5) `ec127a2` — series highlight (dim-others) alongside the existing hide toggle, plus a follow-up fix `08580e0` clearing a stale `highlightedKey` left pointing at a series the user had just hidden; (6) `9abf3da` — click-to-annotate notes (`ChartNotes`, session-only, mounted outside `chartContainerRef` so exports never see them), plus a follow-up fix `277cc0d` correcting a stale-draft mis-attribution bug, a duplicate-DOM-id collision between two open note forms, and making the chart's point markers keyboard-operable (all three found in self-review, not the original brief). Full mechanism and as-built detail: [ADR 038](decisions/038-chart-view-state-editing.md). **Resolution of decisions A-H, matching ADR 038's own accounting exactly:** A (trend-headline rewriting under a zoom) and C/D (visually distinguishing an annotation/disclosure from official data) and E (whether notes appear in downloads) were **resolved by the mechanism this build chose**, not by an independent owner call — suppressing the headline outright, a separate clearly-labelled disclosure/note affordance, and mounting notes outside the export container, respectively. B (bar Y-axis always zero) and F (notes session-only, no persistence table) were built to their **most conservative, reversible default** — always-zero, no schema change — **because the owner had not resolved them by the time this autonomous session ran, not a formal owner decision**; both are flagged in ADR 038 for explicit owner review. G (transparent PNG export) and H (map view) are **untouched, fully out of scope** for this build. **⚠ Superseded (2026-09-08, same day): no PR was ever opened.** The owner returned to the session before the branch was pushed, reviewed the summary directly in chat, and gave standing owner-present authorization to merge — the branch was fast-forward-merged onto `main` and pushed (`e91cdd1..3a92b61`), then deleted (it was never pushed to `origin` in the first place). All 6 pairs above, this ADR/open-questions addendum, and 2 further same-day follow-up fixes (session-89's status-archive entry, items 9-10 area) are on `main`, live in production — not on a feature branch, not pending review. **⬆ DEFERRAL LIFTED BY THE OWNER, 2026-09-17 (session 111, owner present): "Ik wil me nu focussen op het verbeteren van de grafieken … grafieken aanpassen door te chatten." The session-88 "no LLM instruction schema in v1, wait for usage evidence" parking is superseded — there are no users yet to produce that evidence, and the owner made the call. DECIDED + SCHEDULED as the **Chart co-pilot** (ADR [056](decisions/056-chart-copilot.md), spec [superpowers/specs/2026-09-17-chart-copilot-design.md](superpowers/specs/2026-09-17-chart-copilot-design.md)): chat and the existing controls interleaved on one chart, one command log/undo history, one closed vocabulary shared by both doorways, two trust tiers (own data: full freedom incl. aggregate/derive from a fixed set; CBS/Eurostat: selection-only, numbers never through the model). Owner decisions the same day: fixed derived-column set; CBS chart edits persisted in the ACCOUNT from phase 1 (`chart_edits`, [#274](open-questions.md)); input label "Pas deze grafiek aan". Hands-on finding that shaped it: the competitor's chat cannot change styling or annotations and its steps are stateless (a later prompt drops an earlier filter).** **Phase 1 built (session 112, 2026-09-18, branch `s112/copilot-p1`)** — the command log, undo/redo, edit-history popover, in-place title/caption editing, and per-account persistence (migration 034, file-only), all driven by the panel/canvas doorway only; the chat doorway (phases 2–3) is not built. See ADR [056](decisions/056-chart-copilot.md)'s "As built — phase 1" section. **Phase 2 built (session 113, 2026-09-18): the own-data co-pilot — the fixed aggregate/derived set computed by the executor, a Data panel and the "Pas deze grafiek aan" chat doorway on the own-data card, both on the one undo history, persistence keyed by the dataset turn (migration 035, file-only). See ADR 056's "As built — phase 2" section. Phase 3 (CBS/Eurostat chat doorway) is next.** |
| 215 | Product / UX | **Owner feedback, session 88 (2026-09-08, given explicitly in passing — "doesn't mean you need to work on it now") on the chart download feature (`web/components/chart-download.tsx`, #170(3)):** two asks. (1) The "download as vector" option should be a **PDF**, not the current SVG. (2) The PNG download is **~2x too large**, and the owner wants it **transparent background, no source/attribution text baked in — just the chart itself**. Grounded against the current code (read-only check, nothing changed): `PNG_SCALE = 2` is the resolution multiplier behind the file-size complaint; `attributedSvgMarkup()` currently bakes a solid background rect AND a footer attribution text line into BOTH the SVG and the PNG export by deliberate design (the file's own comment: "so both formats serialize the SAME SVG markup ... a PNG and an SVG of the same chart can never show different text" — the attribution-always-present behavior was a considered choice, not an oversight, so removing it for PNG specifically is a real product decision, not a bugfix). | **BUILT session 110 (2026-09-17)**, branch `s110/215-chart-export-pdf-png`, not yet merged. Resolved as two ADDITIVE menu options rather than reversing the existing invariant: the default PNG and SVG exports are byte-identical to before (untouched — 272/272 `chart.test.tsx` + 19/19 `chart-embed-dialog.test.tsx` + 10/10 `user-chart.test.tsx` + 7/7 `messages.test.ts` all still pass, plus `chart-download.test.tsx` grew from 51 to 57 tests, all green, `tsc --noEmit` clean). New: (1) **"Download as PDF"** — client-side vector conversion via `jspdf` (^4.2.1) + `svg2pdf.js` (^2.8.1), dynamically imported only on click, built from the SAME attributed markup the SVG export uses (so a PDF can never show different text than the SVG); a real bug was found and fixed along the way — the existing markup builder emits a duplicate `xmlns` attribute that a strict XML re-parse rejects, worked around via a lenient HTML-parser re-parse rather than touching the shared builder. (2) **"PNG, chart only (transparent, no source line)"** — a new minimal clone builder (no white background, no attribution, no headline, no frame; scale 1 instead of the default scale 2), filenamed `<base>-<syncDate>-chart-only.png` so provenance survives in the filename since it's deliberately absent from the pixels (never guessed when no sync date is available). Full reasoning, alternatives (server-side PDF rendering, a raster-PDF fallback, a shared-builder-with-flags design), and trade-offs (font fidelity, frame/headline dropped, filename-only provenance) in [ADR 053](decisions/053-chart-export-formats.md). **Open assumption carried forward, not decided:** whether the chart-only PNG should keep the journalist headline (ADR 050) when one is set — dropped for v1 on the literal reading of "just the chart itself"; revisit if the owner wants a captioned-but-unbranded variant. |
| 216 | Benchmark / maintenance | **Found session 89 (2026-09-08), live benchmark run during the `PHRASING_MODEL`/`WEBSEARCH_MODEL` cost switch (ADR [013](decisions/013-answer-composition.md)'s as-built addendum): B20 (`reason: "freshness"`, "Wat was de inflatie van vorige maand?") now gets a real ANSWER instead of the expected refusal.** Root cause has nothing to do with the model change: B20's condition is explicitly time-relative ("asked when the loaded CPI table does not yet cover that month"), and the real, live CBS CPI table (86141NED) has since been synced through July 2026 (`gesynchroniseerd op 2026-08-26`) — with a reference date of 2026-08-15, "vorige maand" now genuinely resolves to a covered period. The answer given was fully correct, attributed, and marked `(voorlopig cijfer)` — not a hallucination, and not caught by the hermetic (CI) gate, which replays pinned fixtures immune to real-world data drift; only the supplementary LIVE run (`npm run benchmark:run:live`) surfaced it. | **Not fixed — a benchmark-maintenance task, not a product bug.** B20's `condition` needs a freshness gap that stays true regardless of when CBS's monthly release lands relative to when the benchmark runs — e.g. reference the sync lag structurally (ask about a period that is ALWAYS at most partially covered, like "twee maanden geleden" if CBS's typical publication lag is ~6 weeks) rather than a fixed relative offset that real data can simply catch up to. Whoever picks this up should also check whether other time-relative refusal tasks (any other `reason: "freshness"` rows) have the same staleness risk, and consider whether the monthly maintenance session (CLAUDE.md Conventions) should re-run `npm run benchmark:run:live` periodically specifically to catch this class of drift before it's found mid-unrelated-work. **Live-scorer fix BUILT session 110 (2026-09-17), branch `s110/216-b20-freshness-drift`, not yet merged/deployed — this is a scorer-code change, not a data/product change, so it needed no owner sign-off to build:** `scripts/score-benchmark.mjs`'s B20 check is now DATA-CONDITIONAL on the LIVE path only (`dump.mode === 'live'`; the hermetic path is provably byte-for-byte unchanged — see below). When a live run's B20 record comes back as a real `answer` (not a refusal), the scorer now computes the previous calendar month of the run's own recorded `referenceDate` (deterministic, e.g. `2026-08-15` → `2026MM07`; helper `previousMonthPeriodCode` in the scorer) and passes the task only if the answer is attributed to EXACTLY that period and carries zero fabricated numbers (the existing R1 unbacked-token scan still runs over it, unchanged) — never a guess, always derived from the run's own evidence. When the record is still a refusal, the pre-existing freshness-offer-vs-frozen-key check applies completely unchanged, so a genuinely wrong refusal (e.g. one whose own freshness offer has already drifted past the frozen key) still fails the gate — "covered" is never a free pass for a bad refusal. The report (`benchmark/live-benchmark-report.json`) now carries a `note` on the B20 task result saying which branch applied ("covered -> answer expected" or "not covered -> refusal expected"), so a reader can see why without re-deriving it. Measured: hermetic `npm run benchmark:run` + `npm run benchmark:score` still shows 14/14 answerable, 6/6 refusal/clarify, 0 fabricated, GATE VERDICT: PASS, with B20 now printing `[not covered -> refusal expected]` — proving the hermetic path is unaffected. `npx vitest run tests/benchmark --maxWorkers=1`: 32/32 passing (28 pre-existing + 4 new scorer-teeth tests pinning both live branches: covered+answer passes, a covered/advanced freshness offer on a refusal still fails, not-covered+refusal still passes under live mode, and an answer attributed to the wrong/uncovered period fails). Drift-risk check on the OTHER three refuse tasks (B17 scope, B18 forecast, B19 causal): none share B20's risk — B17's topic (asylum) was never onboarded into ANY loaded table (a scope gap CBS "catching up" can't close, unlike B20's genuinely-loaded-but-not-yet-covered table); B18 asks about 2027, and ADR 017 Decision 2 pins the live run's `referenceDate` to the labelled set's clock (2026-08-15, never the wall clock), so 2027 stays the future regardless of when the run executes; B19 is a causal-interpretation refusal with no time dimension at all. No change made to any of the three. **Not yet run:** the next real `npm run benchmark:run:live` is owner-supervised real spend and has NOT been executed as part of this fix — the fix is verified only against the hermetic pair + the scorer-teeth suite's synthetic live-mode dumps (which build one genuine, reconstructable 'answer' AuditRecord via the real deterministic pipeline — `respondToIntent` with `templateOnly: true`, no LLM call — over the real ingested fixture database at its real freshest loaded month, 2026MM06). |
| 217 | Architecture / LLM harness | **Owner request, session 89 (2026-09-08), given directly in chat after seeing the B8 phrasing gap (#216's sibling finding, ADR 013's addendum): "I want to be model-independent — set up so I can swap in a different model at any time" without a manual prompt-tuning cycle each time.** The real answer already exists in this codebase as an unshipped draft: [#162's `SLOT_PHRASING_ENABLED`](05-data-rules.md) experiment — with the flag ON, the phrasing model writes digit-free prose with typed placeholders (`{waarde1}`, `{periode1}`) and deterministic code fills every slot from the validated result through the same formatters the template rung already proves, so a fabricated OR a silently-dropped number becomes structurally unrepresentable no matter which model is writing — closing exactly the class of gap B8 exposed (an omission, not a wrong value) at the architecture level instead of the prompt-wording level. Revisit alongside #172 (a related but distinct model-tier question, on the intent/rerank side) at the next maintenance session. |

Two very different halves: (1) interface strings — today a hard-coded Dutch/English MIX across components (`Nieuwe chat` next to `Search chats`, the chart card in Dutch) that all has to become translatable; (2) CBS's own words on charts and in answers (measure titles, region names, units, the R4 attribution sentence) — Dutch source data; an English chart needs a maintained word list for those, never an LLM translation (principle (a)/(c): a translation step can invent meaning; a word list cannot). The Dutch CBS answer pipeline itself (intent parsing, phrasing, benchmark phrasing) is NOT in scope of this switch. | **Planned as WP218 phase 4 ([08-build-plan.md](08-build-plan.md)), not scheduled; the largest phase (5–8 days).** Needs its own design round before build: the i18n mechanism (a message catalogue, cheapest first; no library until the catalogue exists), where the choice is stored (URL, cookie, account — ties to #218 C), how the per-chart dropdown interacts with the app switch, and the CBS word-list source/ownership. Owner may pull it ahead of phases 2–3 — ask at the next session start. **Built as WP218 phase 4, session 91 (2026-09-09), ADR 040:** the mechanism is a `lang` cookie plus a typed message catalogue (`web/lib/i18n/messages.ts`) — no i18n library; every chart follows the app language by default and can also be set per chart (rides the ADR 039 presentation mechanism, saveable as an account default), and CBS's own words (units, region names, measure titles, period labels, the attribution sentence) go through a maintained word list in `web/lib/i18n/cbs-words.ts`, never machine translation. Known limitations, all by design: the backend-built prose on a chart (the provisional note, null-cell notes, the definition line, the trend headline) stays Dutch even on an English chart; the definition toggle's label and the source badge follow the app language rather than a chart's own override; "Kopieer als citaat" stays Dutch because it quotes a Dutch answer sentence. Resolved pending merge — a pull request is open for the owner's review, nothing is live yet. |

Migration `028_user_chart_styles.sql` (file-only, owner applies) also creates this table; until it is applied the counter simply counts nothing. (22 characters) can still slightly exceed a ~20-character line budget on a very narrow chart, the same failure mode as this row at a smaller scale. | 224 | Product / architecture / billing | **Embed's "Live" toggle (ADR [041](decisions/041-public-embed-pages.md), spec Part B) is built, tested, and can show as enabled in the dialog for a Pro account (`PRO_ACCOUNT_EMAILS`) — does a `?live=1` embed actually re-render live for anyone today?** No. The render-time Pro check (`web/app/embed/[token]/page.tsx`) must resolve the audit row's OWNER to that owner's email — an anonymous visitor loading a shared embed URL, days or weeks after it was minted, has no session of their own to read an email from — and the only email-lookup this codebase has (`currentUserEmail()`, `web/lib/current-user.ts`) reads the CURRENT session's own JWT claims, not an arbitrary other user's. No admin/service-role Supabase client exists anywhere in this codebase (no `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` in any env file) to build one with. | **Deliberately gated CLOSED, session 93 (2026-09-10), ADR 041:** `rerunLive` calls `hasProPlan({ id: record.userId ?? '', email: null })`, which by `hasProPlan`'s own fail-closed contract is always `false` — every `?live=1` embed silently and permanently falls back to frozen, regardless of `PRO_ACCOUNT_EMAILS`. Safe (no stale/wrong data, no leak), but a real limitation the owner should know about, not a bug. **Resolution path:** build a user-id → email lookup (a Supabase admin/service-role client, or a mirrored `email` column kept in sync by a trigger), then pass the real value into `rerunLive`'s Pro check in place of `email: null` — no other change needed anywhere in the feature. Correctly judged out of scope for this build (new secret, new privileged client, RLS-bypassing surface) rather than improvised. **Scoping pass, session 94 (2026-09-10):** see [session-briefs/2026-09-10-224-pro-owner-email-lookup-scoping.md](session-briefs/2026-09-10-224-pro-owner-email-lookup-scoping.md) — recommended a narrowly-scoped Supabase service-role lookup, no schema change, but also recommended holding off building it until there's a real demo request or #205 is scoped.

**Resolved, 2026-09-12 (branch `embed-live-creator-lookup`) — superseding session 94's mechanism recommendation, not its "worth building now" call (the owner asked for it directly this time).** Design note: [session-briefs/2026-09-12-live-embed-creator-lookup-design.md](session-briefs/2026-09-12-live-embed-creator-lookup-design.md). Built `src/billing/creator-email.ts`'s `lookupUserEmail(db, userId)` — reads `auth.users.email` through the app's EXISTING `pg.Pool` connection (`DATABASE_URL`, the same Supabase Postgres project GoTrue's own `auth` schema lives in) instead of a new admin/service-role client (a new privileged secret) or a mirrored+trigger-synced column (a schema change plus a second copy of personal data needing its own GDPR-purge coverage) — per CLAUDE.md's "cheapest viable mechanism first" rule: no new secret, no schema change, no new library. Fails closed to `null` on any error (no `auth` schema, denied privilege, malformed/unknown id), never throws, never logs the email. Wired into `web/app/embed/[token]/page.tsx`'s Live branch ahead of `hasProPlan`. **Live still cannot be observed working until the owner runs one verification query on the live project** (`select email from auth.users limit 1;`, docs/RUNBOOK.md's "verify the pooler role can read `auth.users`" step) — until then this degrades to exactly the prior always-frozen behavior, nothing breaks. ADR 041's revisit trigger and decision point 10 both carry as-built notes. **➡ Session 99 (2026-09-12): PR #9 (`650d664`) and PR #15 (`8d0f0d4`, the Pro-owner e-mail lookup) MERGED to `main`; Live activates once the owner sets `EMBED_TOKEN_SECRET` and the one-line `auth.users` read check passes (RUNBOOK).** |
| 228 | Architecture / operations | **Could a Live embed silently stop updating because the CBS table behind it got evicted?** | **Yes, in theory, once Live is ever actually turned on ([#224](open-questions.md)) — moot today, but worth recording now, session 93 (2026-09-10), ADR [041](decisions/041-public-embed-pages.md).** `rerunLive` (`src/chart/embed-live.ts`) deliberately runs its query as `{ probe: true }` (a live embed re-render is never a billed/served turn, the #195 discipline) — which means it never bumps `cbs_tables.last_queried_at`, the exact signal `src/ingestion/eviction.ts` anchors table eviction on (`coalesce(last_queried_at, created_at)`). A CBS table kept alive ONLY by live-embed traffic, with no ordinary chat questions ever asked against it, looks exactly as stale to the evictor as a table nobody queries at all — it could be evicted out from under a live embed. After that, the embed would silently and permanently fall back to frozen, with no alert. Nothing to fix today (Live cannot activate at all — [#224](open-questions.md)); revisit specifically once that gate is resolved, since this is a real trap for later that is easy to forget once #224 is fixed and Live traffic becomes real. |
| 229 | Product / UX | **Is "table form is never embeddable" actually true for every chart, or only for the `?form=` query string?** | **Only the query string — a pre-existing, narrower gap survives, found during the final whole-branch review, session 93 (2026-09-10).** `web/app/embed/[token]/page.tsx` correctly refuses a hand-crafted `?form=table`, and the Embed button is hidden while a chart is actively showing its Tabel view (`chart.tsx`'s existing `state.form !== 'table'` gate) — both predate and are unaffected by this finding. But `ChartView`'s own default-form rule (`chart.tsx`: a spec with more than `BAR_LABEL_MAX` (15) series opens on Tabel regardless of `spec.kind`) is evaluated fresh on the FROZEN embed's own render, from the spec alone — independent of whatever form the publisher was looking at when they clicked Embed. Concretely: a >15-series chart, switched to Lijn/Staaf, embedded with chart-type "Default" (which deliberately omits `?form=` so the spec's own default applies) still renders as a Tabel embed. Not introduced by this plan — it is `ChartView`'s pre-existing >15-series default, just newly reachable through embeds. **Not fixed in this build** (out of the `?form=table` fix's scope, which only closes the query-string path): a future session could either have the Embed button additionally check the SPEC's own default form (not just the current view state), or accept table-form embeds for this one narrow case and give them their own export path (the standing reason table form has never been embeddable/downloadable at all). **✅ FIXED, session 110 (2026-09-17), ADR 041 addendum:** the Embed dialog now takes the first option. `defaultFormIsTable` (the exact `spec.series.length > BAR_LABEL_MAX` predicate, moved to `web/lib/chart-view-state.ts` so `chart.tsx` and `chart-embed-dialog.tsx` can both call the identical function without a circular import) is checked against the PRIMARY spec at both `ChartEmbedButton` call sites in `chart.tsx`. When it is true, `ChartEmbedDialog` no longer renders the "Default" radio at all — the reader is left with only "As shown", which `buildEmbedCode` already encodes as an explicit `?form=<current>` — plus a second, independent belt inside `buildEmbedCode` itself that maps a `chartType: 'default'` down to `'as-shown'` whenever `defaultIsTable` is true, so the fix holds even if a future caller re-adds a way to select "Default" in this case. `web/app/embed/[token]/page.tsx`'s own `?form=table` refusal is untouched. Tests: `web/lib/chart-view-state.test.ts` (`defaultFormIsTable` at/above `BAR_LABEL_MAX`), `web/components/chart-embed-dialog.test.tsx` (the "Default" radio absent + `form=<current>` always present when `defaultIsTable`, present otherwise) — 21/21 and 58/58 passing respectively (`npx vitest run web/components/chart-embed-dialog.test.tsx --maxWorkers=1`, `npx vitest run web/lib/chart-view-state.test.ts --maxWorkers=1`). |

NOT independently fixed here: a correct general fix needs the hook to detect the observed element changing underneath a stable ref (e.g. a callback-ref pattern, or a per-render check against a tracked "currently observed" element) rather than relying on `useEffect`'s dependency array — a real, non-trivial change to a hook this ADR's own battle-test already went six rounds on, so it was deliberately left for dedicated attention rather than a rushed fix under review-and-merge time pressure. | **✅ Both trigger paths fixed, session 103 (2026-09-15, autonomous — owner away, PR per [#118](open-questions.md)(b)).** `useElementWidth` now re-checks `ref.current` on every render (no dependency array) against a second ref tracking what is actually being watched, tearing down and reattaching only on the render where the node genuinely changed — the one general mechanism this row asked for, covering the schema-refusal null→element case and the frame-inset reparenting case identically. **A further real bug was found and fixed in the fix itself before shipping:** Next.js's own default is React StrictMode on in development, which mounts every component twice; the unmount-cleanup effect's own cleanup had to reset BOTH tracking refs, not just disconnect the observer, or StrictMode's simulated remount would see "nothing changed" and never create a replacement — a regression test rendering under a real `<StrictMode>` wrapper (not simulated by hand) pins this. Verified in a real browser too (the hermetic dev harness, no live LLM/DB): asked a fixture-replay question, toggled Frame Inset back and forth several times, read the container's actual measured width/height at each step — confirmed correct re-measurement every time and an exact return to the original value on a full round trip, zero console errors. **PR #28 MERGED session 104 (2026-09-16, `d7a4895`, independently re-reviewed before merge).** **Deliberately still open, NOT fixed here** (the row's own resolution only asked for "both trigger paths," these are separate smaller residuals): the one-frame stale-width flash on re-enable (table → chart), the stale `ResponsiveContainer` comment, the area-form export-guard test coverage gap, and the stale `chart.tsx` Style-panel-portaling comment. |

If a future caller ever legitimately renders multiple independent `ChartView`s outside the provider (a future embed page? a multi-chart dashboard?), this would need either mandating the provider there too, or `ChartEditModal` gaining the same one-open-at-a-time awareness `StylePanelOwnerProvider` already gives the app-level state. |
| 245 | Engineering / build & CI | **Owner ask, session 101 (2026-09-13, in chat): "the build/dev experience is getting noticeably slower" — diagnose it.** Two independent measurement passes found the CI gate (8.5–11.6 min) and a full local backend run (measured 776.8s) are ~87% one thing: `tests/billing/ledger.test.ts` boots a fresh in-memory Postgres inside a `beforeEach` — 58 times for 58 tests — when once per file would do (a standalone script measured a 60–100× cost difference between a fresh boot, 2.4–3.7s, and a `TRUNCATE`-reset of an already-booted instance, 32–44ms); `tests/ingestion/ingestion.test.ts` has the same pattern. CI additionally runs the suite as 15–17 sequential per-module steps instead of letting all 148 files share the runner's cores at once. Full diagnosis, a measurement plan, a 3-tier optimization plan with exact snippets, and a ranked first-3-actions list: [session-briefs/2026-09-13-build-performance-diagnosis.md](session-briefs/2026-09-13-build-performance-diagnosis.md) (published as an artifact during the session too). **Three sub-questions only the owner can answer before the CI-sharding tier specifically is worth scheduling:** (a) which wait actually bothers you day to day — the CI gate, the local pre-push block, or something else; (b) is this repo meant to stay public (GitHub reports `private: false`, 0 billed Actions minutes today, but RUNBOOK/STATUS both talk about it as private/costed — parallel CI jobs are free while public, ~+2 billed min/run if it goes private); (c) how many cores does the 8GB machine have, and is `next dev` normally left running while tests run there (decides whether capping Vitest's worker count is worth doing). | **✅ Actions 1+2 BUILT (session 101 continued, 2026-09-13), pushed straight to `main`, verified via real CI + deploy.** Action 1 (`0503a62`): `ledger.test.ts`/`ingestion.test.ts` boot PGlite once per file instead of per test — measured 126.9s→~24-34s and 71.0s→8.25s, full backend suite 148 files/2262 tests unchanged, no leakage. Action 2 (`9132052`, fixed same session by `9e4e7df`): CI restructured into a 3-way `backend` shard matrix + parallel `web` job; the first push broke `web` (root `npm ci` dropped when the job split — ADR 018 point 9's symptom), caught live on run `34750330671` and fixed immediately; re-run `34750430890` fully green (`web`, `backend (1/2/3)`, `deploy`). Real CI-timing comparison (old vs sharded gate wall-clock) not yet measured — check the next few runs' durations before claiming the projected ~4min number from the diagnosis. **✅ Action 3 BUILT (session 110, branch `s110/tinfra`, not yet merged/pushed — a parallel subagent's scoped work, see the diagnosis brief's "Action 3 as-built" section for full detail).** Shared helper `resetTestDb(db, {tables?})` added in `tests/helpers/reset-db.ts`: TRUNCATEs every `pg_tables` row in `public` except `schema_migrations` (cached per db instance), re-seeds the two migration-seeded singletons (`signup_grant_config`, `trial_pot_config`). 40 files across `tests/attachments/`, `tests/billing/`, `tests/catalog/`, `tests/db/`, `tests/registry/`, `tests/threads/` converted from a per-test `createTestDb()` boot (via a literal `beforeEach` or a per-test `withDb`/`withPricedDb` helper) to one boot per file/describe + the shared reset; measured per-file wall-clock (individual `vitest run <file> --maxWorkers=1` runs, one before/one after): **795.7s → 200.3s summed across the 40 files, a 4.0x aggregate speedup** (`tests/attachments/retention.test.ts` 56.0s→3.1s = 18x; `tests/db/migration-012.test.ts` 41.2s→3.6s = 11.5x; `tests/billing/history.test.ts` 74.8s→6.3s = 11.9x; `tests/threads/threads.test.ts` 76.7s→6.9s = 11.1x; full numbers in the brief). Two real correctness bugs found and fixed during the sweep, not just perf: `tests/threads/threads.test.ts` and `tests/threads/dataset-threads.test.ts` each had one test that does schema DDL (`drop table`/`alter table drop column`) to simulate the pre-migration-026 production shape — on a shared instance that DDL permanently poisons every later test, so those two tests were kept on their own private `createTestDb()` rather than the shared one; `tests/billing/creator-email.test.ts` creates an ad-hoc `auth` schema/table in two different tests — added a `drop schema if exists auth cascade` to that file's own `beforeEach` (the generic helper only touches `public`). All 40 files pass individually after conversion; `npm run typecheck` clean. Files deliberately left on the old per-test path, with reasons, are in the brief. **Still not done:** the owner's 3 sub-questions from Action 2 remain open for the CI-sharding tier specifically (unaffected by Action 3); this branch has not been merged to `main` yet — a follow-up session should review + merge it. |
| 246 | Product / billing | **Found during the Pro subscription tier build's own review of Task 6 (wiring web-search/dataset debits into the Pro bucket — [docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-13-pro-subscription-tier.md](superpowers/plans/2026-09-13-pro-subscription-tier.md), [#205](open-questions.md)), 2026-09-14, not an owner ask.** Confirmed by reading the actual queries, not assumed: `src/billing/history.ts`'s `getQuestionHistory` and `src/threads/index.ts`'s `getThreadRows` both compute their displayed `creditsCharged`/`credits_charged` PURELY from `credit_transactions` — each wraps a `not exists (select 1 from credit_transactions d where d.user_id::text = a.user_id and d.request_id = a.request_id and d.reason in (...))` guard (returning `null` when nothing matches) around a `sum(d.delta)`-minus-compensations query that only ever reads `credit_transactions`. Neither query has any join, subquery, or even a comment mentioning `pro_bucket_ledger` (migration 030, this same build). Meanwhile `splitDebit` (`src/billing/ledger.ts`) writes the bucket-funded portion of a debit ONLY to `pro_bucket_ledger` (via `debitBucket`) — when a request is funded entirely from the Pro bucket, `fromLedger` is 0 and `splitDebit` writes NOTHING to `credit_transactions` for that `request_id` at all. | **✅ BUILT session 109 (2026-09-17), branch `s109/246-bucket-cost-caption` (not yet merged — a dispatched build session's work, pending the owner-present session's own review/push per the #118 git-workflow rule).** Both `getQuestionHistory` (`src/billing/history.ts`) and `getThreadRows` (`src/threads/index.ts`) now also sum matching `pro_bucket_ledger` rows — `reason = 'debit'` netted against any `reason = 'compensation'` via `related_entry_id` — added to the existing `credit_transactions` sum; the `null` case now fires only when NEITHER table has a matching debit. The wrinkle this row flagged is handled: both read paths check the turn's plain `request_id` (the base question/onboarding-ack debit's bucket id) AND the two `deriveAddonRequestId(requestId, 'websearch' | 'dataset')`-derived ids (an add-on's bucket leg), since `pro_bucket_ledger` has no `reason` column to filter by the way `credit_transactions` does. Implementation shape: `src/billing/pro-bucket.ts` gained a new batched `getBucketNetCosts` helper (one extra round trip per call, over the whole page/thread's candidate ids, not per row); `src/billing/ledger.ts`'s `deriveAddonRequestId` is now exported for `history.ts` (same module) to reuse directly. `src/threads/index.ts` could NOT import either — its own documented invariant, "this module NEVER touches `src/billing/**`" — so it keeps a local, byte-for-byte duplicate of both the derivation function and the bucket query, guarded by a new "deriveAddonRequestId parity" test (`tests/threads/threads.test.ts`) that fails loudly if the two copies ever drift. Tests: `tests/billing/history.test.ts` 38/38 passing (7 new: fully bucket-funded, split debit, bucket-funded refund, websearch add-on bucket leg, dataset add-on bucket leg — the last one a direct, synthetic query-mechanism test, since a dataset-chat turn never actually shares a request_id with an audit_answers row in production today, marked **Assumption** inline — non-Pro byte-identity regression, read-only/no-write check); `tests/threads/threads.test.ts` 33/33 passing (8 new, the same scenarios plus the parity pin). Also verified green in the same run: `tests/billing/pro-bucket.test.ts`, `ledger-split.test.ts`, `ledger.test.ts`, `gate.test.ts`, `dataset-gate.test.ts`, `tests/threads/dataset-threads.test.ts`, `replay.test.ts` (all pre-existing, unaffected). Root `npm run typecheck` clean. Query-only fix, no migration, no live DDL, no LLM spend. As-built note: ADR [020](decisions/020-credit-ledger-and-billing-gate.md)'s 2026-09-17 addendum. |
| 254 | Product / UX | **Owner-shared research, 2026-09-15 (chat, session 103): a general "what journalists want from a chart tool" summary (external, unsourced-in-repo — web research the owner pasted, not independently re-verified against primary sources), given as context for [#253](open-questions.md)'s later investigation.** Five categories: (1) narrative/editorial control — choosing the story angle, annotation/emphasis on key points, headlines that state the takeaway rather than a bare label; (2) design/branding flexibility — matching newsroom style, simplifying or enriching, fast chart-type switching; (3) data/accuracy flexibility — easy data updates, transparent sourcing/footnotes, context controls (inflation/population normalization, absolute vs. relative); (4) workflow/speed — fast iteration under deadline, low technical barrier with room for advanced customization, export/embed for print/web/social with accessibility (alt text, readable labels); (5) audience/platform flexibility — reworking one visual for web/mobile/social/print, optional interactivity (filters, hover, small multiples) with a clear default view for skimmers. Named comparison tools: Datawrapper, Flourish, newsroom Illustrator/Figma templates. | **Open — checked against what this product already has before treating anything below as a gap; not yet independently re-verified against the cited tools/sources itself.** Already built, mapped directly to the categories above: chart-type switching (Line/Area/Bar/Horizontal bar/Table tabs, WP218 phase 5); annotation/emphasis (click-to-annotate notes, ADR 038); a numeric takeaway leading the card (the chart-card-polish work just built, PR #31 — a headline figure, not yet a journalist-authored headline sentence); newsroom-style presets (8 templates incl. `newsroom` and `social`, `web/lib/chart-templates.ts`, ADR 043) plus auto-detected brand colours/fonts (WP218 phase 3); transparent sourcing (table ID, sync date, licence — R4, on every answer by construction, not optional); export/embed for web and social (PNG/SVG download, the `social` 4:5 template, the `/embed/[token]` iframe route, ADR 041) — **this product's own differentiator the list doesn't have a category for: zero technical barrier by construction** (a chart is produced by asking a question in chat, never a spreadsheet import or manual chart-building step Datawrapper/Flourish still require). **Genuine gaps:** a journalist-authored headline/title (today's title is always the CBS table's own title, never editable prose the reader writes) — **✅ BUILT + LIVE, session 105, 2026-09-16, see [#259](open-questions.md)/[ADR 050](decisions/050-journalist-chart-headline.md)**; context controls: the seasonally-adjusted-vs-raw half — and, more broadly, any of the 20 registered same-measure alternate readings (index level vs. year-mutation, year-average vs. snapshot, etc.) — is **✅ BUILT + LIVE, session 106→107, 2026-09-16, see [#261](open-questions.md)/[#262](open-questions.md)/[ADR 051](decisions/051-chart-alternate-reading-toggle.md)**, shown in chat, dock, and the anonymous trial. The level-vs-%-change half stays open — it needs a new registered series-wide derivation and an ADR 011 revision, not an extension of the toggle mechanism; "easy data updates" in the Datawrapper sense doesn't map cleanly onto this product (most charts answer a live CBS question fresh each time rather than being built once from a static uploaded sheet — the closer analogue, re-uploading a revised file in the "eigen data" attachments feature, is a separate, already-shipped mechanism, ADR 037); small multiples exist (session 79) but a general "explore with filters" mode does not. **The level-vs-%-change half is now ✅ BUILT + MERGED (2026-09-17), see [ADR 052](decisions/052-period-over-period-percent-change.md) (ACCEPTED — the owner delegated its four open questions to the parent session rather than answering each himself; the decisions and reasoning are recorded there).** A new registered `period_change` derivation (src/query/derivations.ts) computes period-over-period percentage change over an already-answered level series for 7 curated canonical measures with no CBS-published mutation sibling (population, housing stock, average home price incl. per-gemeente, bankruptcies, solar production, disposable household income) — offered as one extra entry on the SAME ADR 051 reading dropdown, never a second query, with a cadence-specific label ("t.o.v. vorig jaar"/"vorig kwartaal"/"vorige maand"). **Two logged, deliberately-not-done follow-ups from that ADR:** (a) household income's three alternate income concepts were NOT added to the eligibility list — would need their own real-data check, not assumed from the primary concept's eligibility; (b) `producer_price_index_level`'s own missing ADR 051 alternate (a real companion measure code, `M003288`, was found and verified, but wiring it was confirmed EMPIRICALLY to invalidate the intent parser's recorded LLM fixtures project-wide — ADR 012's documented consequence of any registry change — so it needs its own owner-supervised session that budgets for a real `npm run intent:record` fixture re-record, not a silent registry edit). **How this feeds [#253](open-questions.md):** the map/geo gap in that row is one specific instance of the "design/branding + data" categories here: nothing in this row's research is itself about library or tool choice, but a later investigation of #253 should check candidate approaches against this full needs list (annotation, presets, export, honesty-safe framing), not maps in isolation. **(a) checked session 109 (2026-09-17):** verified hermetically against the committed fixture (`tests/fixtures/cbs/83932NED/{measure-codes,codes-Inkomensbegrippen,observations-page-1}.json`), not live data. All three alternate income concepts share the SAME table (83932NED) and SAME measure code M003239 ("Gemiddeld inkomen", unit "1 000 euro" per `measure-codes.json`) as the primary "besteedbaar inkomen" — only the `Inkomensbegrippen` dims value differs (A043964 primair, A043965 bruto, A043967 gestandaardiseerd vs. A043966 besteedbaar/primary, `src/registry/defaults.ts` lines 316-327). (i) Level, not already a %/mutation: confirmed — same measure/unit as the primary, a plain euro amount, no percentage concept among the three. (ii) Cadence/coverage: confirmed identical to the primary — filtering the fixture on `Measure=M003239, Inkomensklassen=T001226, KenmerkenVanHuishoudens=1050010` (the registry's own pinned `defaultCoordinates` for this table) gives exactly 14 annual (`JJ`) observations per concept, 2011JJ00 through 2024JJ00, for all four Inkomensbegrippen values including the primary. (iii) No CBS-published mutation sibling: confirmed — `measure-codes.json` lists only 5 measures for the whole table (household count, persons/household, persons-with-income/household, Gemiddeld inkomen, Mediaan inkomen), none a growth/mutation measure, for ANY Inkomensbegrippen value — the same absence ADR 052 already established for the primary (rule (b)) holds identically for the three alternates since it's the same table and the same 5-measure list. (iv) Non-null, computable: confirmed — every one of the 14 yearly values per concept carries `ValueAttribute: "None"` (no censoring) and is strictly positive (25.6-96.2 in units of 1000 euro across the 3 alternates), so `derivePeriodChangeSeries`'s contiguous-period and zero/negative-base guards (`src/query/derivations.ts` around lines 294 and 307) would not refuse. **Verdict: all three alternates are ELIGIBLE, on the same grounds already accepted for the primary.** What a build session must do: `PERIOD_CHANGE_ELIGIBLE_KEYS` (`src/registry/defaults.ts` line 602) is a `Set<string>` keyed by canonical registry `key` — but these three concepts have no canonical key of their own, they exist only as `alternates[]` entries reached via `buildAlternateReading` (`src/answer/respond/respond.ts` around line 505), and today `isPeriodChangeEligible` is checked ONLY against `parse.intent.target.key` for the PRIMARY result (line 521) — `buildPeriodChangeReading` is never called on an alternate's own built result. Extending coverage needs either (a) an optional per-entry eligibility marker added to each `alternates[]` object, or (b) a parallel (key, dims) keyed set, plus a call to `buildPeriodChangeReading` inside the `chartAlternates` loop for each successfully built alternate. Tests to extend: the ADR 052 eligibility-list test enumerating `PERIOD_CHANGE_ELIGIBLE_KEYS` and the `src/chart/period-change.ts` test suite (confirm exact filename before editing). **Fixture invalidation check: NOT invalidated.** `PERIOD_CHANGE_ELIGIBLE_KEYS` is imported only by `src/chart/period-change.ts` (verified — no other importer in `src/`) and is never read by the intent-parser prompt builder; `src/answer/intent/prompt.ts` (lines 136-143) only emits each measure's `key`, `definitionLabel`, `everydayTerms`, and `alternates[].label` text into the ADR-012 registry-derived system prompt, and those label strings for the three income alternates already exist in the registry unchanged. A period-change-eligibility extension implemented as a new, separate marker (not a new canonical `key`/`everydayTerms`/edited `alternates[].label` text) changes zero prompt bytes and would not trigger an ADR-012 fixture re-record — only a change that added a genuinely new canonical measure key or edited existing vocabulary text would (as already happened, separately, with the still-open `producer_price_index_level`/M003288 follow-up (b) above). **(a) BUILT session 110 (2026-09-17):** implemented option (a) from session 109's own note above — an optional `periodChangeEligible?: true` marker added to `CanonicalMeasureAlternate` (`src/registry/types.ts`) and its runtime mirror `AttributionAlternate` (`src/query/types.ts`), round-tripped through the existing `canonical_measures.alternates` JSONB column unchanged (no migration). Set on exactly the three `average_disposable_household_income` alternates (`src/registry/defaults.ts`). `src/answer/respond/respond.ts`'s registry-alternates loop now also calls `buildPeriodChangeReading` on an eligible alternate's own resolved result (via a new `validated: ValidatedResult` field added to `buildAlternateReading`'s return shape, `src/chart/alternate-reading.ts`) and pushes an extra `chartAlternates` entry labelled via a new `composeAlternatePeriodChangeLabel` helper (`src/chart/period-change.ts`, e.g. "primair inkomen — procentuele verandering t.o.v. vorig jaar"). Full addendum: [ADR 052 § Session 110 as-built addendum](decisions/052-period-over-period-percent-change.md#session-110-as-built-addendum--254a-alternates-eligibility). **Measured:** `tests/registry/registry.test.ts` 16/16, `tests/chart/period-change.test.ts` 12/12 (2 new), `tests/chart/alternate-reading.test.ts` 6/6, `tests/answer/respond-pipeline.test.ts` 27/27 (1 new byte-identical-envelope assertion), `tests/audit/envelope-key-manifest.test.ts` 9/9, every other `tests/chart/*` and `tests/registry/*` file green, `npm run typecheck` clean. No live registry sync run (no DB in this worktree) and no new LLM fixture recorded (none needed — see the ADR addendum's "Not done, and why"). Built on branch `s110/254a-period-change-alternates`, not yet merged. |
| 253 | Product / engineering | **Owner request, 2026-09-15 (chat, session 103): investigate whether a different charting/mapping library would serve this product's map/geo visualization needs better than what's used today, after this session ends.** Raised while discussing chart quality against the owner's own `checkdecijfers-3d-demo.vercel.app` reference site and a general Chart.js resource list (the `chartjs/awesome` GitHub repo) the owner shared. Confirmed live in this session: Chart.js is not installed anywhere in this repo (`web/package.json` and every `node_modules` checked directly) — the product uses `recharts` (`web/package.json`) for every real chart, and this session's own 3D map DEMO (see the 3D-municipality-map-demo plan) uses `three` plus a hand-rolled TopoJSON decoder, not Chart.js. | **Open — deliberately deferred to a later session, not this one (owner's own framing: "let's doc that to investigate after this session is done").** What's already settled, so a later session doesn't re-derive it: (a) Chart.js (and most other high-performance chart libraries) render to `<canvas>` by default — pixels, not DOM text nodes — which conflicts with this product's core honesty mechanism for the MAIN answer-pipeline chart renderer (`web/components/chart.test.tsx`'s `scanForUnboundDigits` walks the rendered DOM's text nodes to prove every visible digit is a spec string bound to a real CBS cell, R1/R6); swapping the general-purpose chart renderer to a canvas-based library would need that whole audit mechanism rebuilt, not just a styling change — not recommended without a much stronger reason than "nicer defaults." (b) Most of the `chartjs/awesome` plugin list's chart TYPES (treemap, sankey, word-cloud, venn, network graph, parallel-coordinates) would fail this product's own honesty rules for the same reason pie charts are refused (docs/05-data-rules.md, principle (a)/(c)) — they visually claim a complete whole, a flow, or a relationship that a single CBS query result doesn't actually contain; most of the ecosystem breadth doesn't translate into usable capability here regardless of library. (c) The one genuinely real, unmet gap the comparison surfaced: a proper MAP/geo chart type (`chartjs-chart-geo` was the concrete example) — this is directly the same gap [session-briefs/2026-09-02-session-69-chart-ux-research.md](session-briefs/2026-09-02-session-69-chart-ux-research.md) parked ("the most natural lay question the product can't chart today... large, re-raise if real region-set demand shows up") and the reason this session's 3D map work is a fictional-data DEMO, not a real feature (docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-15-3d-municipality-map-demo.md — no "every region's value" query capability exists yet). **Recommended scope for the follow-up investigation, once a real region-set query capability exists (not before — there is no real data to map yet):** compare Chart.js + `chartjs-chart-geo` (canvas; would need its own honesty-scan strategy since the DOM-text-node approach doesn't apply to canvas output), a hand-rolled D3/TopoJSON approach (what the demo already proved out, SVG-native, same honesty-scan mechanism as everywhere else), and a dedicated mapping library (e.g. Mapbox GL, deck.gl, Leaflet + choropleth) — on: SVG-vs-canvas honesty-scan compatibility, bundle size, real interaction quality (pan/zoom/hover on ~342 municipalities), and whether it can reuse the demo's own topojson decoder/projection work (`web/app/bevolking-3d-demo/topojson.ts`) rather than starting over. **Owner decision, 2026-09-16 (session 104, chat): after reacting to the 3D-demo quality gap and seeing it fixed (session 104, [#258](open-questions.md)), the owner said to move focus "back on the standard graphs instead of storytelling" and chose to run this investigation next session, rather than more 3D-demo/storytelling work.** Flag for that session, not resolved here: this row's own "Recommended scope" above says explicitly **not before a real region-set query capability exists** — check whether that precondition is actually met before assuming a map/geo library comparison is the right next action; if it still isn't, [#254](open-questions.md)'s own "genuine gaps" (a journalist-authored headline mechanism, context controls like level-vs-%-change/seasonally-adjusted/normalization) are directly-actionable "standard graphs" quality work with no such precondition, and may be the more honest match for what the owner actually asked for. **Verified session 109 (2026-09-17): the precondition is STILL NOT MET — checked the actual code, not docs.** `StructuredIntent.regions` (`src/query/types.ts`) is always an explicit, non-empty array of CBS region codes the intent parser (LLM) echoes from the question — there is no wildcard/"all members of dimension" value anywhere in the type or the resolver. `resolve.ts` (the region-handling block, roughly lines 279-303 and 536-598) only ever accepts named codes: the one region-comparison mechanism, `derivation: 'max'` (`deriveMax` in `src/query/derivations.ts`, "B14-style ranking... needs at least 2 source cells"), requires "exactly 1 period and at least 2 regions" but nothing resolves "every gemeente" — every test's region arrays top out at 2 explicit codes (`tests/query/*.test.ts`, e.g. `['GM0363','GM0599']`). The one SQL primitive that WOULD be reusable already exists: `src/query/run.ts` (~line 389) runs `region_code = any($4::text[])`, an array-IN query already fetching N named regions in one round trip — the missing piece is entirely upstream of that, not in the database layer. Two upstream gaps, confirmed by grep: (1) nothing populates that array with "all members" — the intent parser only ever echoes codes/names it read in the question (ADR 012: hallucinating a code is structurally impossible, but so is emitting a list of ~342 codes it was never given); (2) no resolver function reads all codes of a `RegioS`-kind `GeoDimension` from `dimension_labels` — the table that already stores every code+label per table+dimension (`migrations/001_ingestion_schema.sql` line 78), populated at ingest from CBS's own `{Dim}Codes` response including a `DimensionGroupId` (`src/cbs-adapter/parse-v4.ts` lines 86-107) that already distinguishes region LEVEL (landsdeel/provincie/COROP/gemeente) — so a future "all gemeenten" query would not need to guess from `GM`/`PV` code prefixes, the ingested data already carries the grouping. Small multiples (`web/components/chart-small-multiples.tsx`, reuses an already-built `ChartSpec`'s own `series` array) and the 3D demo (`web/app/bevolking-3d-demo/fake-data.ts`, every record and the dataset itself marked `fictional: true`, no real query behind it) both confirm the same gap from a different angle: they render whatever series/regions were ALREADY resolved, neither one resolves "all regions" itself. **#253 stays correctly blocked. Minimal scope for a future region-set query capability**, so a build session doesn't re-derive this: (1) a new resolver function, e.g. `resolveRegionSet(db, tableId, geoDimension, level)`, reading all `dimension_labels` codes for a table's geo dimension at one `DimensionGroupId`/level, to use INSTEAD OF relying on `StructuredIntent.regions` for this one query shape; (2) a new `IntentTarget`/`IntentDerivation` shape the intent parser can emit WITHOUT enumerating codes itself (e.g. a `regionScope: 'all_gemeenten'` flag) — keeping the LLM's job classification, never code enumeration (principle a); (3) reuse the existing `region_code = any(...)` SQL as-is once the array is populated server-side — no new query primitive needed; (4) the R5/R9 honesty rule this must not skip: `deriveMax`'s ranking language ("de meeste/hoogste") is only truthful when the array genuinely covers every member of the level (R9, `docs/05-data-rules.md`) — a region-set query that silently dropped members (pagination, a stale/partial ingest) must refuse the ranking claim rather than answer over a partial set, extending the same "refuse rather than guess" instinct `deriveMax` already applies to ties; The registry entry's `definitiveStatuses` goes `[]` → `['Published']`, so an unflagged Eurostat cell no longer renders "always provisional" while `c` (confidential), `:`/`n`/`z` (not available / not significant / not applicable) and every other flag stay provisional STRUCTURALLY — no flag equals the one definitive string, so a future unknown flag is provisional too. Conformance F3 additionally fails any per-cell status not in the manifest's `declaredPeriodStatuses`. **No migration needed and none written:** `observations.status` is plain `text not null` with no CHECK (`migrations/001_ingestion_schema.sql`; nothing later alters it) — verified, not assumed. No DB, no network. Mitigation (b) (a stated human convention — re-checking `ls migrations/` against `main` before opening/updating a long-unmerged PR) was deliberately NOT built here, out of this row's scope (not code) — still worth doing as a habit alongside the automated check, not a substitute for it. |
| 264 | Engineering / data | **Found registering the first real Eurostat table, session 107, 2026-09-16/17 — not an owner ask.** ADR 048 D7(a) (migration 032, `cbs_tables.doi`/`cbs_catalog.doi`) states DOI "populated at catalog/registration time for a Eurostat table once E1's ingestion actually registers one" — but `registerTables` (`src/ingestion/pipeline.ts`) never sources or writes a DOI value at all; the column is nullable and simply stays `null` for every table, `eurostat:tipsbd30` included. Distinct from, and found alongside, the `source`-column bug the same registration surfaced (that one IS fixed — see [#249](open-questions.md)). **Researched session 108, 2026-09-17 (subagent, docs-only): the DOI source is now VERIFIED, not just guessed at.** Eurostat mints one DOI per dataset, pattern `10.2908/<CODE>` (code uppercased), per Eurostat's own "Anchoring of datasets" user guide — confirmed live by fetching the public, unauthenticated DataCite REST API (`GET https://api.datacite.org/dois/10.2908/<code>`) for three real datasets, including `tipsbd30` (the one table already registered in prod): 200 response, `state: "findable"`, title matching what's already in this product's records. The resolver path (`https://doi.org/10.2908/<CODE>` → 302 to the Eurostat Data Browser page) was also confirmed live. Neither the Catalogue "table of contents" endpoint nor the Statistics API expose it — DataCite is the only verified programmatic source. One caveat, read but not stress-tested: Eurostat's own wording ("foresees to assign DOIs to all... datasets") implies the rollout may not cover literally every one of the ~10,331 catalog entries yet; only 3 were spot-checked, no counter-example (a dataset with no DOI) was found or sought. | **✅ BUILT session 109 (2026-09-17), branch `s109/264-eurostat-doi` (not yet merged/reviewed by the owner).** `registerTables` (`src/ingestion/pipeline.ts`) now constructs the DOI deterministically (new module `src/eurostat-adapter/doi.ts`, `eurostatDoiFor` = `10.2908/<CODE uppercased>`, zero API calls) then verifies it out-of-band against the public DataCite REST API (`verifyEurostatDoi`, never the request path, never throws) before ever writing it — `doi` is written only on a 200/`findable` response, left `null` on a 404/timeout/network error/unexpected exception, exactly as this row recommended. Scoped to `source = 'eurostat'` rows only via `sourceKeyForTableId`; a CBS row never gets a doi and never triggers a fetch. Hermetic tests added to `tests/ingestion/ingestion.test.ts` (findable→doi set, 404→null, throwing fetch→null+registration still succeeds, CBS never fetches, re-registration is a no-op skip) — all pass, no real network touched. `cbs_catalog.doi` was checked, not assumed, and is intentionally NOT touched: `registerTables` never writes `cbs_catalog` at all (that's the separate `ingestCatalog`, whose upsert has no `doi` column and whose source data doesn't expose one). ADR 048 gets a Third As-built addendum under D7(a) recording the new DataCite ingestion-time dependency. **Backfill of the already-registered `eurostat:tipsbd30` row is an owner RUNBOOK step, not run by this session** (no live DB writes from a dispatched session): new idempotent script `scripts/backfill-eurostat-doi.ts` (`npm run backfill:eurostat-doi`, dry-run by default, `--apply` to write), documented in docs/RUNBOOK.md's WP30c E1 section. Still open for a future session: actually running that backfill against production, and merging/reviewing this branch. |

| 320 | Product / engineering | **Found session 127, accepted by the final ADR 057 review (Minor 6).** The 50-live-publications-per-author limit (`publishOwnChart`, ADR 057 §1) is a soft cap: it counts the author's current live publications, then upserts — not one atomic transaction. Two publish requests from the same author racing each other at exactly 50 could both pass the count check and both insert, landing at 51 (or briefly higher) rather than being hard-capped at 50. | **Assumption:** accepted as-is — no money, no privacy impact (it only ever loosens a soft limit by a couple of rows in a narrow race window), and the fix (a transaction-scoped count, or a DB-level check) is not worth adding until it is ever actually hit. Revisit only if an author is observed racing past the limit in practice. |
